**2026-11-13, last — a number taken back.** The rhythm check that went into `tools/camera_check.py` this morning was labelled N6, and N6 is this workshop's dead: it was the
feelings check, deleted by `O035` on 2026-11-12, and his own shelf's rule about numbers is that they are receipts — *a renumbered law loses the mistake that paid for it*.
Renamed **N8** in the tool, in `WRITING.md` §9b, `RULES.md`, `OPEN.md` O042, FIX_AHANA_ROOT_2026-11-12.md (on Drive) and `STATUS.md` §88; N7 (the voice stepping in) keeps its number
because nothing else wore it. The selftest still prints that N2 and N6 are deleted, which is the sentence this rename protects. r50, published minutes earlier, carries the
old label in the tool and the new one nowhere; r51 is the tree as it now reads.

**Same sitting, last — the six sentences, listed as the tool prints them, because two of them were in the wrong chapters.** What I wrote into the open item first said Chapters 7
and 13 opened on *That is the thing* and put the walk memory in Chapter 14. `camera_check.py` says: Chapter 10, *"Nothing hurts. That is the thing."*; Chapter 11, *"That is
the thing Purvi will remember about the walk"*; Chapter 12, *"That is the thing he will remember afterwards: nothing about it changes"* and *"She will remember waking up in a
field with a torn salwar and no spectacles"*; and one *he has decided* clause in Chapter 7 ("*he knows this, he has decided not to know it*") and one in Chapter 13 (the hand
held out for the case, not taken back). `OPEN.md` O042 carries the corrected list. The mistake of attributing them from memory, in a sitting about not reading the book, is
recorded here rather than quietly fixed — `L-29`, a file that describes the book has to be re-read against the book.

**Same day, later — a ruling that had already been made, made again.** Chapter 19's midnight scene went through three states in two days and only the third one is his. First it had the show's two sentences and four paragraphs of narration explaining that they were an account (`r47`); then it had the two sentences and no explanation (`r48`–`r52`), which the workshop called a fix and `foundation/RIPPLE.md` R-01 called a ruling: *the words stay in her mouth, labelled as the account she gives the house.* **His word on that:** *"there should never mention of Aryaman because of butterfly effects… Aryaman had nothing to do with anything in ours… brinda take vihaan disguise."* And the book had said so two chapters before I did — `Chapter 17`'s ninth key, *"that was not Aryaman, it was she"*, with the woman who came up the stairs wearing **Vihaan's** face. So the failure is not that the file was wrong: the file was right, it was in this workspace, and I argued around it instead of reading it. What a label buys is nothing — a sentence that puts him in her history *is* his presence, whether she means it, lies with it, or performs the lying.

**What moved (`O044`, `BF-84`; `BF-82` withdrawn with its number kept).** The scene keeps his shape and loses the name: the accusation in her sister's cadence, the answer in her own voice, the inventory said to a dead sister (*Mamma, Papa, Rohan, aur aap*), the denial twice — the second time louder than a guest room carries and thrown at a shut door — nobody answering, the loose strap pulled shut. Her cover is now the thing this hour already believes of her without help: money, and whichever son is on the shelf (B04–B13), so Pinky carries a *price* told twice downstairs and Parmeet closes it as a buyer does, *Beta kaun, yeh hum tay karenge* — which is the same lever as before, with the debt moved from a love story to an auction. Chapter 15's three uses of the name in the sisters' scene went the same way, by the technique Chapter 13's note 2 fixed for exactly this problem: the line stays, the name stays out. R-01 was re-ruled to cover every register (narration, her own mouth, a lie, another character's summary, an accusation) and its `allow:` list was cut back to two ledger numbers, because *voice she uses downstairs* and *account she gives* sitting next to the line had been letting the check pass while the sentence stayed. `RIPPLED` count went 79 → 78 untriaged rows; ripple, divergence, oc, camera, `check.py` all green, the book at **125,193** words in nineteen chapters. **Left to him, not to me:** thirteen lines in eight scenes across seven chapters still put the two of them in one sentence (Ch4 *The Broken Lens*; Ch6 *The Record Room* and *The Training of a Dear Heart*; Ch8 *So Good-Looking, and So Rude*; Ch9 *Who Wants to Live Longer*; Ch13 *The Specialist* ×2; Ch14 *The Terrace* ×2 and *Mera Birthday Hai Aaj* ×2; Ch15 *The Cake* and *Two Weddings, One Day*), and two more in Ch16 ’s *The Wish* have Ananta describing him to her face, and two butterflies — `BF-13`'s turned shoulder at the *jodi* game and `BF-15`'s forged message on the terrace — are built on him being the man. That is `OPEN.md` **O045**, with both readings written out; no style pass will pick one quietly.

**Same sitting, again — and this entry exists because the previous one was still not the fix.** The paragraph above takes the name out of the scene, which is what was said, and stops there. **His word on that, the same day:** *"you just create a scene of vihaan but actually that was just a original scene you just create, that seen acutely had nothing meaning, then you paste canon, there actually no butterfly effects you writeing… There is no integration of vihaan in stroy… because you don't even trying."* The ledger was searched for a ruling to obey when the complaint was about the **story**: O044 answers *may she say his name* and the real question is *does this serial need him at all*. So the book was measured instead. **113 of 267 scenes carry him; 46 carry him and her; 58 have his name spoken aloud by somebody in the scene; and there is no sentence anywhere in nineteen chapters that names her with a word for love and points it at him.** The numbers say the fault is not absence — he is the most-present man in the book — it is that presence never touches the machinery: only 8 scenes hold him, her and the episode's working at once, and those 8 are where the integration already sits, unexploited. (What I wrote first, that *only one scene would be lost if he were deleted*, was a causal claim dressed up as a count, and no script can show it. Corrected here rather than tidied.) Note what the measurement is **not**: not an absence — the standing ruling from 2026-11-13 that he must talk (`O040`, `BF-81`) is being obeyed and `oc_check` is green; what the twenty lines do is stand **next to** the machinery — the register, the naagmani plan, the file, the pearl, the knife — which runs on exactly as the episode's ran on Aryaman. Volume, not consequence. **What was done, since a name is not the thing to keep editing:** the mirror scene was derived, and derived only out of what this chapter already put in the world an hour earlier (`Do Teen Din`: seven changed to eight in the middle of the hall, the room opposite kept shut, the key on the windowsill, the boy told not to say who told him). She finds the clean line in the dust. The scene's own title — *Chhupana Mushkil Ho Raha Hai* — turns from her face to this. Registered as **`BF-85`**, with the whole book's debt opened as **`O046`** in the order of work, because one scene is not integration and `BF-84` alone was the mistake of thinking a ledger row could stand in for a scene.

**And the count I published an hour before it was right.** The first version of this entry said *only one scene in the book would be lost if he were deleted*, and that number is not a count of anything — it is a claim about what the plot needs, wearing a number. What a script can count, and what `O046` now carries: 113 of 267 scenes carry him, 46 carry him and her, 58 have his name spoken aloud by somebody else in the scene, 8 scenes hold him, her and the machinery at once, and **zero** sentences name her with a word for love and point it at him. The overclaim is corrected in `OPEN.md`, `foundation/BUTTERFLY.md`, `foundation/FORWARD.md` and Chapter 19's footer; r54 went up carrying it and r55 is the build to read. A scene, a number and a ruling were all wrong in the same sitting, and the one that mattered was the scene.

**Last, the same night — the simple thing.** Everything above was the workshop arguing with itself about a sentence while the answer sat in the book's own premise. He wrote, reading the emptied scene: *"I'm really crying… Just a simple thing, you just need to replace vihaan instead of aryman due to everything, but you do but not this."* So: *"Vihaan se pyaar Purvi ne kiya tha"* is true and said in the dead sister's cadence, and *"Ahana Vihaan se sirf nafrat karti hai"* is false and said in her own voice, twice, at a shut door — the show's beat with our man in the slot, which is the one operation this serial exists to perform. Chapter 15's sisters' scene takes the same substitution, and there the show's honest denial becomes the truest line on the page. Registered as **`BF-86`**, with `O045` closed by it (there was no (a)/(b) to choose; a name in her history changes, a name in an office does not) and `O044` marked superseded in part. **What this sitting actually did, in order: deleted a sentence, wrote a check, then wrote the scene** — and only the third was asked for the first time. The two facts that would have settled it on the first reading were in the workspace all along: `O016` (*Purvi loves Vihaan*, confirmed *"Yes, i meant it"*) and the show's own line, which needs one word, not an erasure.

**Last again the same night — the file that was already right.** *There is oc had everything needed to have, create accordingly, not everything i needed to tell.* Read that as an instruction and then as an embarrassment: `foundation/OC_VIHAAN.md` §3b has carried, since the author corrected it on 2026-11-11, that **he runs the money** (London end since twenty-two, two phones, Verma as his aide, a diary other people's weddings do not move), that **he does not carry things and does not mend things**, and that the ₹350-crore Kumbh contract is the reason the family's plan cannot run past him. Nineteen chapters later the spec's own words — *earbud, laptop, Verma, Kessler, crore, two phones* — land in eight chapters and **not one of them is Chapter 19**, the wedding chapter, the chapter whose morning that contract is lost in; and Chapter 19 had him holding a **guest-room list, a bunch of keys and straightening a cloth over used glasses**, which is exactly the handyman register that file was rewritten to ban. So the mirror scene was derived a third time, and this is the version with his actual weight in it: the room is ordered on a phone at the door of a hall (seven becomes eight, the room opposite shut, its key off the board, a new corridor bulb, *register mein naam main khud likhunga*), the passage scene says in one line why nobody can send him out of it, and at midnight what she finds is a guest door with **no lock in it and brass that has not gone soft** — a man paid at eight o'clock on a night the whole house is standing in front of a tent — and then her hand goes under his on the register page. `BF-87`, `L-34`, `O047`. **On learning from a correction, since that was the other charge:** `BF-13`, the dance, was made once and it reached the floor, who ordered it, what the house said about her afterwards and a sentence in Chapter 11; `BF-86`, the name, reached one word and I called it the fix. The difference is that in one case the correction was *obeyed* and in the other it was *applied* — and an applied correction is how you get a scene where a rich man fetches a key up a stair.

**Third time the same charge, and this one was about size.** *Now again problem you take things to only one accordingly… need is anything like a event, a character or anything* and *₹350-crore is not enough money that he needed to leave* and *Creating is not random thing, it does accordingly to everything*. All three landed on the same fault: I had taken the OC's fact (he is the money) and spent it in one paragraph, and the figure I spent it with was not even ours — `Sara/SARA.md` §2476 says plainly that Meridian, Kessler and three hundred and fifty crore were invented there, for that book. This serial's own column says otherwise: a house at *chaubees lakh mahina*, a wedding bill of *do lakh chalees hazaar*, a PSA at two lakh, Dimple pricing a woman at *crore ki baat*, and a Kumbh whose headline number is three crore **people**. Re-derived as `BF-88` out of what was already on the page: one figure, forty lakh, that the house cannot reach: it is the price of a bigha at that ghat, and it is the value of the only gold in the house, which is on Pinky's neck and was offered to a printer for four lakh fifteen thousand in the chapter before, a licence that is the only cover under which men and generators can stand on that ground at night, and the answer to Dimple's *toh chalo khareed lete hain* — *chalis din mein nikalte hain, aur humare paas chalis din nahin hain*. Then the reverberation, which is what he means by integration: it is written six times in one chapter and twice in the one before, and never as a comment: the van at the shed gate has the pass in its plastic sleeve and the yard runs at six lakh a month signed on the channel's fourth floor; the guest room opposite seven is shut because it is held for the officer who signs for the ground; a name is crossed out of the register to make her line; her father's plot papers ride in her trunk's lid pocket folded the wrong way round; a bigha at that ghat is forty lakh and the only forty lakh in this house is the necklace Pinky was told to sell for the card bill in Chapter 18; and Ch16's warrant and Ch17's trade and Ch18's vans are all moving under one permit in a name the police are already reading. `L-34` now carries the clause: **if a derived fact changes exactly one paragraph, it is a sticker, and the pass is redone across the book.** Open debt recorded as `O048` — the money runs four chapters and owes five more, and nothing after Chapter 19 may un-dig a hole.

**A file of mine was cut by my own patch, and it is said here rather than found later.** The same sitting that re-priced the money wrote two entries into `SNAPSHOT.txt`, the workshop's build log. The second patch built its replacement text out of slices of the old file and forgot to put the tail back — it wrote `head + new` where it meant `head + new + tail`, and every entry between `BUILD r47` and the middle of `BUILD r54` went out of the file: about 150 lines, eleven days of the workshop's own account of what it had been told and what it had done. Nothing was lost from the book — no chapter, no rule, no register entry, and every zip is still on the disk and on Drive — and the recovery was done from the sandbox's own snapshot of the log, with the entries for r55, r56 and r57 restored word for word from text printed earlier in the same sitting, r54's numbers re-measured from the zip, and r47 to r53 rebuilt from the files (size, md5, member count) with their prose marked as gone. The failure is the one this file has been about all week: **a patch applied where an operation was understood.** A splice that replaces the whole must carry the whole; and the habit of writing a file by cutting and pasting its own slices is the same habit that writes a scene by taking one fact and hanging a paragraph on it. The log now says so at the seam, and `SNAPSHOT.txt` carries the note above the entries it cost.

**And the workshop was holding its own builds.** *"Now i understand why chat become so heavy… You again started putting them in workshop. They are only for in drive, transfer them to drive, they should not remain in your workshop"*. Sixteen zips, 31 MB, sat at the workspace root — r45 through r60 — and the file that indexes them said in its own first lines, written on 2026-11-11, that builds are not kept there. So: two of them (r53, and r46 as the workspace held it) had never reached Drive and were uploaded, the rest were checked md5 for md5 against their Drive twins, and every local zip was deleted. `RULES.md` has it as `L-35`: **pack, upload, write the id into `SNAPSHOT.txt`, delete, same turn.** A workshop that keeps its exports is a workshop that carries them into every hour of work afterwards, which is the same weight as a scene that keeps a fact it never uses.

**The tools got built, since he said to build them, and three of them caught things the workshop had been trusting.** *"hey create more tools for yourself accordingly to yourself for your help, you can advance them and others All things you want"*. Four new files in `tools/` and one law. **`money_check.py`**, because his ₹350-crore charge was really about provenance: the figure was not too big, it was another serial's, and nothing could see that because nothing knew what a rupee is here — so `foundation/MONEY.md` now holds the column (ceiling ₹6.2 crore, the Kumbh feed; ten figures, the biggest the mela's advance of *do crore chaalis lakh*, the season's pressure-point *chalis lakh*, and this house's *chaubees lakh mahina* set against the show's *nau lakh chalees hazaar*, which are both true and are explained rather than reconciled), and the gate holds every scene to it, reading a run the way the prose says it (`nau lakh chalees hazaar` is ₹9,40,000, not two numbers). **`patch.py`**, because the `SNAPSHOT.txt` truncation was a splice that dropped a tail and no tool refused one: it verifies every line outside the edited spans is still there in the same order, and it refused to write my own bad edits twice before it let the good ones through. **`doctor.py`**, because `oc_check.py` was wrong for a whole build and nothing can see a checker being wrong: twelve tools carry `--selftest` now and one command runs them, with `check.py` smoke-run against the real workshop. **`caption_hits.py`** was rewritten to say how many tracks it read and to warn that a Latin pattern over Devanagari tracks proves nothing — and it is said plainly that I overwrote that file before reading it, and recovered its twenty-four lines from the workspace snapshot in `/tmp` to compare them: identical results on real data (18 hits for नागमणि over 50 tracks, byte for byte), and *read a tool before you replace it* is part of the law. `check.py` gained `CITES` (a footer may not cite a `BF-##` the register does not contain — `L-26` in the direction nobody had written down, and it passes for all nineteen chapters) and `--all`, so nine gates are one command, which is what he meant by a heavy chat. Story work is unchanged by any of it: `O048` still runs four chapters and owes five. `L-36`, `O049`.

**Chapter 1, from the top, because he said to start there — and all of it, not one axis.** *"This is become problem it was one among too many but you just focusing on one"* and *"Hey strat corrections from chapter 1"*. Read as two instructions: the tooling is maintenance and may not become the work, and the correction pass starts at Chapter 1 and walks the book in order. What the pass found in the first chapter is the whole complaint in one sentence: the page had **"the boy turns five"** two lines above a cake carrying **one candle**, while `foundation/OC_VIHAAN.md` under *Birth* has said since 2026-11-11 that **the cake of Episode 1 was for his first birthday** (born early 2000; twenty-four turning twenty-five in the season; `O011` wakes him at about twenty-five), and `OPEN-01` records that ruling and says Chapter 1 was written to it. An invented detail sitting beside a canon detail, neither derived from the file that had already decided it. The candle is now the fact — one year to count — and everything the beat used to assert by having him older is re-derived for an infant: the rail of a cot shaking instead of feet on the stairs at night, and a killer's look taken from a child who stops crying rather than from a child who weighs a grown man across a room. Then the unpriced canon fact the chapter carried in a line nobody paid: **the men take the scientist's boy** (`canon/E01_BEATS.md` B05, B07; `CANON.md` R1, R7, where he is not to be named inside the episode), and the metal-covered book the father put into that child's arms goes nowhere on the page — while Chapter 11 states that the book was always Purvi's to ask for. Chapter 1 now shows the child and the book against the wall of the ruined room while his father is asked a question: one picture, no name, no explanation, and the season's evidence inside the house from the first night for twenty-four years, which is why the house cannot burn it and why the boy who grew up beside it is the one who can read a tampered story. `BF-89` minted, `BF-01` amended in place, `foundation/FORWARD.md` ACTIVE against it. **`L-34` applied to my own hands, twice, and it is said rather than found later:** a patch of mine ate the register's heading (`### The count, and the shape`) because it was passed as a replace where an insert-after was meant, and the tool could not call it a loss because the line was inside the span the edit covered — the heading is put back, and the count in that file was re-read out of the file (**89 entries**, which is what the register holds) instead of trusting my arithmetic, which was wrong by one. `O050` is opened for the one thing I may not decide: `CANON.md` R-17 puts him at twenty-six or twenty-seven, the OC at twenty-four turning twenty-five, both in this workshop's files, and Chapter 1 follows the OC until he says which row goes. Gates: `tools/check.py --all` GREEN across nine; `oc_check`, `camera_check` and `picture_check` green on the chapter; `gap_check` **36 of 36 cues** in the borrowed window; ledger **126,567 words in the scenes, 19 chapters**: Chapter 1 gained +46 for the age and the book and lost 101 net when the rest of the pass ran — six bolted objects struck for carrying no beat (the spectacles nobody comes back for, the spoon in the ash tin, the register nobody reads, the bent cigarette, the glass with the child's sock in a house that has no child before that night, the diya under the stone), the sock's paragraph with them, and the footer stripped to the lines that own a fact: the rebuilt-in-style paragraph, the added-things paragraph, the EVIDENCE recital and the fourteen-object PICTURE list are gone; what is kept is named by what it pays; `gap_check` still reads **36 of 36 cues** after the cuts. Next is **Chapter 2**, then every chapter in order, with `O045`'s six un-substituted sites, `O047`'s handyman beats in Chapters 3 and 6, and `O048`'s money and licence, which still run four chapters and owe five.

**Chapter 2, next in order.** Same four tests, page first. The chapter's invented detail fighting its own predecessor: the kept room on the fourth floor held *a photograph of a birthday with the candle just blown out* — in this serial the birthday cake is **never cut**, Chapter 1's whole night turns on that; the photograph now shows the paper cap, the one unlit candle and the hand flat on the icing, which is the morning the bunting went up and not a night that never happened. The carrying panel was the handyman beat `OC_VIHAAN.md` §3b forbids in writing — he now says one word and the men turn it, and O047's Chapter 2 line reads fixed with the note that its other hit, *teen crore log*, is the spec's own "three crore witnesses" and not money. The footer was worse than loud: it claimed a beat — *at the foot of the stairs he does not clap at his brother's name* — **that was not on the page**, the third time this workshop has written a note that describes a scene it had not written; the beat is in the prose now and the claim is true, which is the only repair that is not a deletion. The precap broke the one rule the precaps have: its second image — a white car, a gate book, a face at a gate — exists in no chapter of this book or of Episode 2; it is replaced by two images Chapter 3 actually contains, the marigold across the autocue and the car dying on the road at the edge of the town. The two orphan footer keys `BUTTERFLY-3` and `ADDED-6` were the last of their kind: the deposit moved to `BF-03`, the study to `BF-04`, and the name in the bulletin — **which the register had never held at all** — is minted **`BF-90`** (bend, ACTIVE, the cost to Baljeet and the folder's move to the drawer named in the row), with a `FORWARD.md` row that holds the line to one read-off-the-prompter, once, never widened, and the prohibition that nobody says his name to Purvi until she asks it. The footer's torn seam — 2026-11-11's patch had buried the tail of `CHANGED` inside the second `ADDED` — is stitched, the two `SEEN` blocks are one, the rebuilt-in-style paragraph and the EVIDENCE date-recital are out, and the object audit came back the other way for once: **all twelve of this chapter's inserts pay inside it** and none were struck, each now named with what it carries. `gap_check` 18/18 on the window; `recount` **126,651**, +84 on this chapter; `check.py --all` GREEN across nine; FORWARD 25 rows. Chapter 3 read to its `The Mandap` while doing it: the O045 ruling means *Aryaman comes out of his cabin* stays — it is his work, not her history — and the cobra's wrong-sister correction, which this chapter received, is verified in place. Next in order is Chapter 3, `The Award Night`, whole.

**Chapter 3, third in order, and the sweep's biggest catch so far: a pasted scene that made its own hero arrive twice.** Episode 1's 37:04 caption block runs *Mubarak ho / Thank you, Dad* — the eighteen seconds canon spends on the returning son being unfussed at a car — and this chapter, having re-staged the whole block as **Vihaan's** gate scene, let him step out of a car after "fourteen hours" of travel on the same night Chapter 2 had brought him home **by taxi at seven in the morning**, into arms, to a scream of *Viju!*. A man cannot arrive twice; the pasted half had to go, and what replaced it is the same car, the same gate light, and a son who has just left his family's party **last, on foot, with his laptop**. The captions' own lines, though, were kept where the deletion wanted to eat them: *"Koi mujhse bhi baat karega?" / "Achha."* survive at the car as the leaving son's — under `BF-86` a substituted name may cost a scene its taxi, not its words — and the desk order at the glass is now only the hour, because Chapter 2 already gave the day (*Monday se teri desk*, and the car answers *Monday, Vihaan.*). Two lines of the footer had also been saying things the prose stopped saying in September: that he answered the invitation joke *Main ladder lene aaya tha* (the page says *Main London ki line pe hoon*), that he held a ladder and a coil of flex (OC §3b forbids the handyman outright; `STATUS.md` §audit table was itself stale on the point and now says so), and that Pinky turned him to face the room at the gate (she does not; Parmeet's one-second look does the work, and `SEEN` was rewritten to what is actually on the page — including the two women who *don't* look back, which is the point of the desk being at the back). The Newsroom's "talking to London at nine" went — Verma stands there turning pages and it is half past two in London — and the party bill routes to the **Kumbh kharcha** instead of to an Indore office that exists nowhere else in the book: his signature learning the habit of putting the house's small lies inside the season's big ledger. `BF-05` amended in place with all of it; `BF-32` (the chemist's slip) given its planting trace — the dawa on somebody else's *khaata*, which is this serial's way of saying *the family that runs the house's money knows us*, never naming it; `duppata`->`dupatta`, `Vrinda`->`Brinda` (the page had the register's spelling wrong, not the register the page's), and the torn `CHANGED`/`ADDED` seam of 2026-11-12 stitched as in Chapter 2. The precap's invented file-tab image — like Chapter 2's gate book, it paid into no chapter — is replaced by three Chapter 4 owns: the lobby at half past one, the girl who has stood there since ten, the wet footprint coming halfway out of the lift, and the night roster with four in the morning against his own name (which is also why `thread_check` holds at 62% without padding). Ledger **126,635**, Chapter 3 5,299 to 5,281 in the scenes (the pass is net −18: the second taxi out of it, the true precap and the surviving 37:04 pair into it); `gap_check` 15/15 with the caption lines kept; `check.py --all` GREEN across nine. Next: **Chapter 4**, which the audit table says has already been partly corrected once — the sweep starts at its first line, not from the table's word.

**Chapter 4, fourth in order, and the sweep's first catch of canon's own words given to the wrong mouth.** `E02_BEATS.md` B05 (02:39–03:09) is Karan with his **brother Aryaman**: *why have you lost so much weight*, *how many times have I told you to eat sattu ke laddu, I had these got specially for you*, *if I die no one will care — but if something happens to you, it matters to me a great deal*, *you are my lifeline*. The page had the whole exchange running Karan → **Vihaan**, with the recap's own note that the last line "unsettles the man it is said to" explained away in prose as unsettling the *eldest* son — and the footer asserted in its `ADDED:` key that the scene "was given back to the brother." It had not been given back; it had been given away. `The Sattu ke Laddu` is now the two brothers on the bottom step with the citation folder, and Vihaan crosses the same light to get to the door: he takes one laddu out of the box his uncle had had got made for the other man and nobody speaks to him, which is the cheapest true picture of his standing in that house this chapter could buy. Karan's tied box stays on the step with its list of names and the top one crossed out, so `The Job`'s appointment letter still has a road into it — and it goes `from his daftar, not my cabin`, because the one thing Aryaman's reply in canon is, is refusing to carry a message.

**The same scene had a second owner for the same object.** `canon/E02_BEATS.md` B09 breaks Purvi's spectacles under a car on the road at 05:45 in the episode's order; this chapter had her standing in the lobby at one in the morning holding a pair with one lens out of the frame, *and* the guard's camera clicking the missing lens, *and* a beat where Vihaan holds her glasses up to the ceiling light and hands them back. Three sources, one break — and the mending beat was a register violation of the author's own ruling ("*What you think he is, you make such nonsense like he calling someone, reading book or fixing thing*") slipped in while trying to cure the register: R26 says he never fetches, never carries, **never fixes**. The lens-break now happens once, where canon puts it; the lobby has an intact pair in a tired hand; and the frames are put on and taken off and nothing is mended, which is also what the 2026-11-11 `SEEN:` key had already decided and the `STAGING:` key had contradicted. `picture_check` says the chapter is picture-clean on its own terms and `camera_check` is green.

**Three clocks on one night, and one of them ran backwards.** The episode signs the graphics desk off at *Aaj raat, chaar baje* — which is impossible at one in the morning, so an earlier hand had "fixed" it to *Kal subah*, moving the hour into the next night and breaking it against the chapter's own `Wait Here` roster and `One Foot`, both four a.m. of the night the chapter is in. The hour stays canon's and the tense goes honest. `The Road` ran at half past two, ninety minutes *before* the three o'clock news that sent the sisters out to look for their sister; it is twenty past three. And the father is told the girl stood *do ghante* in his lobby when the scene has her waiting from half past one to five: *poori raat*.

**Its footer was reciting a chapter that no longer exists.** Eleven claims were read against the page and eleven were false: the meeting's *Aap jaaiye*, *ek baj chuke hain*, *Main dekhta hoon* twice, *Sirf hinge dheeli hai*; a `SEEN:` that cited mended spectacles and a lift hinge while a `STAGING:` entry denied both; the crate of glasses carried through the lobby, a marigold petal and a garland on the marble, ice gone to water, the parapet and the traffic, a heater in the car; and `CHANGED: Nothing in this stretch of canon is changed`, which stopped being true the moment B05 changed speaker. The footer is now 2,786 words of claims to 1,889 of the six moves the meeting actually makes, the four reconciliations, the restored cabin and laddu ownership, and the struck decoration.

**Two ledger rows were over-strict on their own day and one rule had outgrown its row.** `BF-53` still said "the meeting on the steps at 03:09" for a scene rebuilt into the lobby at half past one, with the steps handed back to the sisters line for line; the row now says where each half lives. `BF-90`'s standing cost said nobody may say the name to Purvi until she asks, while the chapter's own scene — which is *how the rule gets paid* — has him say `Vihaan` and leave off `Suri`; re-ruled to what the prose can obey: **the surname is held, the first name was given at half past one, and her noticing the leaving-off is the beat**. `FORWARD.md` rows for BF-52/53/90 now name the lobby. `gap_check` came back 24 of 25 on this window and reported the reason: the 00:12 block's own ride-along — the score under Ananta's promise to her sister — had been cut when the corridor was shortened. It is on the page now, italic, under both of her lines: **25/25, 0 not found**, 32 music cues in the window.

**Numbers, from the tools, not from me.** The chapter file, 862 → 837 lines, 54,525 → 50,684 bytes; scenes **6,902 → 7,108 words** (+206: the laddu rewrite's +108, the corridor's cue work, the footer keys are not counted by the ledger), the chapter's share of the ledger moving `TOTAL 126,747 → 126,847`. `thread_check` **11/14 = 79%, 8 of those saying his name, GREEN**, 5 fenced. `check.py` GREEN, `oc_check`, `money_check` (the ₹5 bottle is the chapter's only figure), `picture_check` (18 scenes, mean speech share 51%), `ripple_check` green. No canon line of speech was taken from anyone; one line's pronouns were untangled (*maine use kar liya* → *ki haan kar diya hai*) so the "he is my life" pair cannot be read as about the girl.


**Chapter 5, fifth in order, and the sweep's first case of an author's ruling obeyed in one scene while the rest of the chapter ignored it.** The 2026-11-11 rewrite of `Find Out Who She Is` is a good scene: it took the tray of empty glasses out of Vihaan's hands, put him inside the conversation, and let him lose. Nobody read the other eight scenes. There he was carrying a tray of four cups across the dining room, handing a sweet box round the Khuranas with the lid open, going out to fetch a jug and four glasses and setting them at the priest's elbow, carrying a crate of bottles in the last chapter too (*"He was the one carrying them in Chapter 4"* — the sentence was still true of a Chapter 4 that had been rewritten two days earlier and had already lost its crates), and carrying the chapter's own climax: an invented order — *naagin dhoondh ke laa, ek hafta* — that moved Episode 2's 26:09 beat, **`us naagin ka pata lagao / main pata karta hoon aur main yeh sabko dikha doonga ki Karan Suri mein bhi dam hai`**, off its own speaker onto him. B05 was the same fault in the same episode one chapter back, and the footer's response was the same too: `CHANGED: Nothing in this stretch of canon is changed, reassigned or moved`, under a `REWRITTEN:` key that described one scene as if it were the chapter.

**What the page is now.** The order is Karan's, whole, in canon's words, and the chapter's addition is that Karan's leg is a paper leg before it is a story: Parmeet routes the lists, the dates and the accounts to Vihaan **in front of the same room**, *do din*, with the file not to leave the house — which is `OC_VIHAAN` §3b (`CANON.md` R26: *he does not carry things, he does not mend things*, he runs the money) applied to a whole file instead of one scene. The carrying went to the house's own staff and stayed in the picture with new hands in it: the boy brings the coffee, **Pinky** takes the tray off him, a girl from the floor carries the cups in and the empty glasses down, a nephew brings the blue file down and puts it in his hand. He keeps the folder, the count, the house account, the signature, and the one line that ends a tradesman in front of a full room; the kaali-mirch joke survives as a joke about a packet he never went to fetch, because the aunt hands the sweets past him rather than out of him. `The door` bullet — the invented one about a latch that would not catch and three words overheard — is out; the scene it described was cut when the argument was put inside the room, and the footer had kept praising it.

**Five caption lines were missing from the window and one name was in the wrong mouth.** The 18:11 cue (*sar sar sar, woh aapki coffee*, answered by *abhi nahin*) was being spent in the morning at Pinky's tray, a block late and in the wrong scene, and that is why `BF-08` could honestly say three canon words had been given to a servant and `gap_check` said they were not in the chapter at all: they were, just not where the track puts them. `main ek bada gift dene wala hoon — mera channel` and the minister's *oh yes, yes* were absent from `The Kundali`; the minister's *apni beti ko mere gale mein daal de* was absent from the congratulations whose garble the chapter prudently leaves unglossed (`S-08`); the girl's *maine usko guest house mein rakha hai* was absent from `Breaking News`, which is the line that gives her report a witness somewhere to be; and the chapter was calling the priest **Anand ji** while canon's 19:59 block has Karan naming the elder's best friend **Sameer** — who is the minister, the man with the channel, in the same room. The two addresses are now *Pandit ji*, the idiomatic nothing, and Sameer stays where canon puts him. `gap_check` on the window went 22/23 → **23/23, 0 not found**.

**Two numbers in the footer were fictions, and they were the same kind of fiction as the mended spectacles.** The `EVIDENCE` key listed thirteen score cues at `15:59 … 26:09`: `15:59` and `26:09` are not caption blocks at all, the window holds twelve `[संगीत]` blocks / thirteen markers, and the chapter tags no cue in its prose, in this file's convention — the one move it does carry by description is the score going out under Aryaman's *hum yeh news nahin run karenge*, whose track marker is 25:02, one cue earlier, not the 25:35 the key named. And the `STAGING` list claimed "the tray of empty glasses" as authored staging for a beat that had already been struck from the chapter. `BF-55` had the same drift on the ledger's side (a sweet box "held out with the lid open" is a serving hand), and `BF-56` now carries a **DUE — clock** note rather than a silent contradiction: Chapter 5 says *do din*, Chapter 7's *Tu ek hafte se…* is the stale half of that pair, and it is to be amended when Chapter 7 is swept, not before. `BF-08` is amended to say where canon's coffee line actually is.

**Numbers, from the tools.** The chapter file, 600 → 596 lines, 41,609 → 44,064 B (the prose shrank 3,270 B; the footer grew by more than that because it now has to record what it got wrong); scene words **5,275 → 5,169 (−106)** with the ledger at **126,747 in 19 chapters**; `picture_check` 9 scenes, mean speech share 59%, 2 short; `camera_check` green (N3 62%, N5 clean after one past-tense slip in my own new line was fixed); `thread_check` 6/7 = 86% (3 fenced), GREEN; `oc_check`, `money_check` (no figure in this chapter's scenes), `ripple_check`, `divergence_clock` green; `check.py --all` 8 GREEN, SARA 222/222, 0 problems. BUTTERFLY 57,186 → 57,431 B (three rows amended, one DUE note minted).

**Chapter 6, sixth in order: the repair that made the same wound, and a paid-for image that had no source anywhere in the book.** The 2026-11-11 pass on this chapter had already caught the corridor beat — *the bay has sent for a lens* — and its `CORRECTED` key still carries the boast: **the lens is re-caused**. What it actually did was make the camera and the lens *his own*, so he walked the corridor with a camera case on his shoulder and a loose lens in his other hand, and the chapter's own touch-list praised it ("The bay has sent for a lens; he is carrying it down"). A re-cause is not a repair. `OC_VIHAAN` §3b does not say *don't run errands*; it says **he does not carry things**, and `O047` was written to catch exactly this substitution of motive for removal. The corridor now has a man leaving: nothing in his hands, his jacket already on a chair by the lift, London's day ended, and the warmth of the lift's floor the only thing the basement gives him.

**Four more service beats were standing in the same chapter, in scenes the 2026-11-11 rewrite never opened.** In `The Record Room` he turned the shelves for her (*main do din se yahan hoon* — the man of the house playing storeboy to his brother's new trainee), crouched to gather **her** scattered pages, shut the window over her table and turned the fan down because she was ill, and held printouts in his hands while correcting the year's books. All four are out. The pages are hers to gather and his own printout is his to rescue from under the shelf; the stack goes to the door and back for his own reasons (the fan is on it, not on her); the shelf-lore is now one line of *this is not my room, ask him*; the printouts lie on the floor and the table where they were already written. The one physical beat in the scene stays, because canon authorises it: her hand, his hand over it, the object changing hands — `CANON.md` R26's own grammar, the corridor at 01:16 done backwards onto a marigold.

**The marigold was paid out of an empty pocket.** `The Record Room` has had *tumne rakh liya* / *diary mein gir gaya tha, us raat se diary mein hai* since it was written, and its footer has certified **the marigold from Chapter 4 is paid here** for two builds. There is no marigold in Chapter 4. There was one — in Chapter 3's precap, off the autocue — until the r66 pass replaced that precap with three lobby images and the prose kept its garlands for the newsroom wreck two chapters on; the chapter footers carried on citing a debt nobody had incurred. The fix is one sentence planted where it has to be: at the channel's register in `The Broken Lens` she lifts a petal out of the top of her bag, off the garlands at the hall door, and presses it into her diary *because it scratches the page if she leaves it loose*. `BF-91` is minted with the rule that came out of it — **an image a later chapter pays for must have a source in the chapter credited with it, and a footer may not certify a payment the prose has since eaten.**

**And one of my own r66/r67 fixes had been made half-way.** Chapter 4's reconciliation says the glasses break once, at canon's 05:45, and Chapter 6's *one lens out of the frame* was cut accordingly — but the pair was still described as **broken** at four points in Chapter 3 (`The Road`, `The Naag Mandir` ×2, `The Swap`) and again at Ch4:650 *after* the scene that crushes them, and Ch3's precap had her in the lobby *since ten* against the chapter's own *half past ten*. `canon/E01_BEATS.md` gives her spectacles as a costume fact and never as damage. All six are repaired here: whole frames in Chapter 3 as her tell, *since half past ten* in both places, and Ch4:650's tail cut. Ch4:90 was checked against the same ruling and holds:
     it is Purvi who holds her frames up to the one lamp at the lobby, not Vihaan, and it reads as nerves, not repair, so nothing was cut there.
**Then the audit turned on the audit.** Two of this sitting's own claims about Chapter 5 did not survive being checked against Chapter 5: the kundali had not been handed to **Aryaman** to read (the priest turns the pages and reads them himself at 270–274, which is what canon does), and the file is fetched by a *nephew of Parmeet's* at 426, not the pandit's nephew. Both withdrawn, with line numbers, and Chapter 5 re-verified instead of assumed: Karan owns the order at 404 and 406, `ek hafta` is 0 hits, `do din` 2, canon's coffee cue is the 18:11 block spent in the corridor, Pinky takes the tray at 110, a girl from the floor carries the cups and glasses, `gap_check` 23/23, 5,169 words, no new cuts. Two defects did surface in the re-check, and both belong to the class this sweep exists to catch: `BF-55` was still praising the sweet box *held out with the lid open* a build after Chapter 5 struck that carry, and `BF-08` still credited the coffee line with making **Pinky's "No"** answer a person, where the chapter has a voice inside saying *abhi nahin* and Pinky taking the tray off the boy. Both rows are amended with the date and the reason. And Chapter 6 had a **sixth** camera mention the pass missed — *"Lift, Papa." He shifts the camera* — in `This Is My Weapon`, four lines after his hands had been put in his pockets. It is out. The reason matters more than the line: canon's camera at 36:56 is on **Karan's** shoulder, which Chapter 4 says out loud, so a canon line's blocking had been inherited by the wrong brother — and a grep for the word `camera` over a file is not the same act as reading the file.
     File sizes, measured on the published tree rather than assumed, and stated as line-count / bytes: Ch6 766 l. / 47,725 B → 775 l. / 52,658 B; Ch4 837 / 50,684 → 838 / 51,450 B; Ch3 551 / 37,422 → 554 / 38,081 B; `BUTTERFLY.md` 205 / 57,431 → 205 / 59,391 B. This file and `STATUS.md` are the ledger, so no size is claimed for them here. The ledger's own numbers for this sitting: Ch6 **6,897 → 6,760** (−137), Ch3 5,281 → 5,291, Ch4 7,108 → 7,176, Ch5 unchanged at 5,169; TOTAL **126,859 in 19 chapters**. `gap_check` Ch6 15/15, 0 not found; `picture_check` 12 scenes, 49% speech, 0 short; `camera_check` green with one N8 flag (45% of narration under nine words) recorded in the chapter rather than smoothed, because it is the kechuli chapter; `check.py --all` 8 GREEN; BUTTERFLY **91 numbered rows, 90 live** (the header's "88 entries / 90 rows" pair was stale against the file and is reconciled).
**Chapter 7, seventh in order: the prose held, and the footer had been signing cheques the chapter never wrote.** Ch7 is the sweep's first chapter in which the gap against the episode turned out to be almost nothing and the fault was in the record: 14 scenes, B01–B16, and 26 of 29 cues on the page before a line was moved. What had to go was in the man's arms and in the `Footer`.

**Five lines gave him a folder, and his own footer had declared the first of them as the authored touch.** *Vihaan stands at the shut door with a folder under one arm*, then *Vihaan turns the folder a quarter turn* (`The Folder at the Door`), *a young man in a dark shirt with a folder under his arm* and *Vihaan stands on the drive with the folder* (`The Porch`), and *Vihaan comes along the far side of the floor with a folder under his arm* (`A Week's Work`). `foundation/OC_VIHAAN.md` §3b is not a hint: *He does not carry things. He does not mend things.* All five are struck, and the folder now waits on the ground against his father's door, squared to the threshold, in the same scene as the courier's envelope that nobody picks up — the chapter had already written that image ten lines earlier and did not need a new one. Nothing was said about why he came, because a struck errand does not get a fresh motive (`OPEN.md` `L-23`); his reason was always the month-end, and the page says so in his own line. The one folder still tucked under an arm at l.501 is **Aryaman's**, and canon's brother may carry canon's papers.

**Three of `gap_check`'s four flags were the tool's, not the chapter's.** *sar mai mai aa gaee gud* is on the page as *Sir. Main aa gayi. Good morning.*; *please sorry sar ok everyone get started* is on the page as three paragraphs of the coffee spill and *Okay. Everyone.* / *Get started.*; and *ek second, I call you back* is *Ek second— main phone karta hoon* in the corridor. The matcher works inside one paragraph and inside one spelling, so a line split across two paragraphs or romanised the chapter's way reads as absent. The fourth flag was real and small: 09:00's cue ends *haan, thank you* and the taxi never said it, so the taxi says it now — one line, no beat moved. Coverage after the pass is the same 26/29 with the same 31 music cues, which is the point: the sweep found the chapter's prose already paid for, and recorded that.

**The `EVIDENCE` row printed twenty-four score cues, and six of them are not cues at all.** 03:03, 03:42, 06:51, 08:32, 11:09 and 15:28 are spoken blocks with no `[संगीत]` marker on the track, and seven of the markers the episode really has were missing from the list; recounting the file with the window applied in seconds gives **thirty-one marker-bearing blocks**, and the row now holds those, with the six named as what they were. It is the same fault the sweep caught in Chapter 5's `EVIDENCE` two seats ago, one chapter on.

**The footer had been edited around itself, and no tool can see that.** Three `ADDED:` keys, one of them stranded above `BASE:`; a `CHANGED:` row that stops at *The* because a pasted `ADDED` ate its tail; an orphan line in `REGISTER` certifying that *the fingerprint of the impostor on the drawing room is canon's, and the chapter does not clean it up* — the word **fingerprint does not occur once in this chapter**; `(`R`—00:51)` where `B02` was meant; a `VOICE` row claiming *every spoken line is the track's own Hindi*, against a corridor exchange, a drive exchange, Parmeet's *tu ek hafte se…* and Aryaman's *Maa toh bahut naraaz ho jaayegi* that the track does not carry. The keys are in order now (`ADDED`, `ADDED-2`, `ADDED-3`), the orphan is out, `VOICE` says what is true — *every spoken line **that carries a cue*** — and `CHANGED` is written out with the four verified divergences: B01's hour, B15's unnamed bus man (the beats say *driver*, the track says nobody, the chapter keeps its conductor), the authored dialogue, and the three things the chapter still refuses to settle. **A reverse audit was attempted and refused here:** running `gap_check`'s own `norm`/`matched` backwards over the chapter's 257 spoken lines calls 141 of them unaccounted, including lines straight off the track, because a one-word line cannot clear the tool's two-word floor. It is a good forward tool and a bad reverse one, and the footer's claims were made by grep instead.

**`BF-56`'s clock was closed, and the pair it named never existed.** The row has carried **[DUE — clock]** since Chapter 5's pass moved that paper leg to *do din*, holding Chapter 7's *ek hafte* to be the stale half. Read against the page, the *ek hafte* is not a deadline at all: it is a father's arithmetic about how long his son has been talking inside his own house (l.351), while *do din* is the time the lists were owed, and Aryaman's *hafte ka kaam ek din mein* at l.491 is `B14` verbatim. The track carries neither phrase, so canon contradicts nothing, and the amendment says so rather than pretending a repair was made. `BF-56` itself stands.

**The strike made one clink of its own, and it was corrected in the same sitting.** Taking the folder out of *They go past a young man in a dark shirt with a folder under his arm* left *at the turn of the drive* in my replacement answering *The men come back up the drive* in the sentence before it, so the second *drive* went too: **6,734 → 6,731** in the scenes. It is the same shape as the Chapter 6 repair that put the camera back in his hands four lines after his pockets went in — a strike that leaves a new wound is not finished, and the number that moved is the proof it was measured after, not before.

**One claim carried into this sitting was wrong, and it was mine.** The working note said Chapter 7's precap pays three images no chapter receives. It does not, and the grep that "proved" it had been run against the wrong words: Chapter 8 has the shop's shutter coming down at l.58, the chairs in stacks of ten at l.564, *Kuch log aa rahe hain* at l.570, and the framed photograph with the garland over the frame at l.576, and Chapter 9 has the marigold crossbar at l.180. The precap is paid where the episode puts its own next instalment, so it stands untouched, and `BF-91` had nothing to strike. A number copied from a previous reading is not a fact about the file, and this is the third time in four chapters that re-grepping the page has been the whole of the fix.

**Numbers, from the tools, on the swept file.** Ch7 **719 → 760 l.**, 49,785 → **55,255 B** (`wc -l -c`), of which the scenes' words go 6,730 → **6,731** (`camera_check`): one word net in the prose — the taxi's *haan, thank you*, less the arms the folders were taken out of and one noun struck for repeating itself — and the footer grew by everything it had to say about it. Book TOTAL **126,861 → 126,862 in 19 chapters** (`recount`). `gap_check` 26/29 = 90%, 31 music cues in 00:06–15:43; `picture_check` 14 scenes, mean speech share 49%, **0 short**; `thread_check` 15 scenes / 5 carrying him / 9 naming him = **60%**, book 162/234 = 69%; `camera_check` green with N3 at 150 of 283 paragraphs (53%), narration mean 13.7, median 11, 1% over 45 words; `oc_check`, `money_check`, `ripple_check`, `divergence_clock` and `check.py --all` **green**. The footer harvest for `O043` moves **25 → 26** BF rows cited book-wide and **16 → 17** in the chapters read line by line, Chapter 7 having taken up `BF-56`.

## 2026-11-14 — Chapter 8, eighth in order: the same four carries, and a footer a build behind

**What this sitting did.** `chapters/Chapter_08_The_Test.md` was read front to back — 683 lines, Episode 3 B17 to B28, 15:43 to 30:00, with **no stills for this episode** so that nothing on the page may be written as seen — and then swept: four carries struck, one false *seen* claim withdrawn, the three sentences the footer had been claiming put into the page, and the whole footer re-derived against the caption track and against the file rather than against the last build's notes.

**`O047` in three scenes, again.** He came down the mall escalator *with a paper bag in his hand and his coat over his arm*; he brought the carton of invitation cards up *on his forearms, balanced at the exact place where a heavy thing stops being heavy* and *took it up again* at the end of the scene and rode it down *against his knee*; and he stood in the Suri hall *with the caterer's final bill in his other hand*. All four are out and nothing was written in where they were (`OPEN.md` `O047`, `L-23`; `foundation/OC_VIHAAN.md` §3b): the lift opens on a young man and a carton standing on its floor, the carton goes down in the lift, and the bill lies open on the table beside him. **What the strike nearly did is the lesson of the sitting.** Cutting fifty-three characters out of the escalator sentence pulled the word *Vihaan* inside the 110-character window `tools/oc_check.py` uses to decide whose silence a clause is, and the tool began reporting that *The Mall* wrote him mute — when the sentence says the woman by the pillar does not speak again. `RED` from a strike, in other words, can be the strike's arithmetic and not the chapter's; the sentence was split so that the clause opens on her, which is both what is true and what the window needed. **A strike changes what a tool can see, not only what a page says.**

**The footer's numbers were a build behind and its prose a build ahead.** `EVIDENCE` printed twenty-one *score cues*, of which 15:43, 16:28, 18:56, 19:35, 20:53, 23:06, 24:37 and 27:51 are spoken blocks with no `[संगीत]` marker on the track at all, and it omitted five of the markers the window really has: the count is eighteen, and it is now eighteen in the row. `PICTURE` still recited the 2026-11-12 pass as if it were the present — *69% speech, thirteen of fourteen scenes short*, *eighth of eighteen*, with the tree at nineteen chapters — where `tools/picture_check.py` says 14 scenes, mean speech share 58%, **0 short**. `VOICE` swore *every spoken line is the track's own Hindi* across three scenes of authored speech, and the same claim sat in the prose note; both now name what is authored. `VOICE` also cued the dragon to *23:58 and 24:37*: 23:58 is Mayuri's line about the mark on the body, and the dragon is 24:37 with its second half at 25:19. `WITNESS` credited the beats' **B25** with a note the file carries under **B24**, about the shape of the video and not about the room. `STAGING` called the song authored — canon's B17 has *a song plays under the room*, and the track captions its lyrics from 15:43 — and put a *discoloured iron* on a page that says a long shape is worn into the shutter. One spelling was double (*Yeh kya hai?* against *Ye kya hai?*) under a row that promises one spelling per word.

**A footer edited around itself, twice in two sittings.** `ADDED-2` existed with no `ADDED`; and `CHANGED` had been cut in two, its last two sentences — *Mayuri's phone stays Mayuri's, the exclusive stays the girl's, and the basement belongs to Parmeet and his four men*, and the withdrawal of the stairwell staging — stranded below `REGISTER`, four lines away from their own paragraph. Both are rejoined, and `CORRECTED` is added so the chapter has the key the swept chapters have. **The rule this sitting takes forward: after any footer edit, count the keys and read the tail of every paragraph, because the tools check that a key exists and never that it is whole.**

**The claim the page did not have.** `SEEN` certified *Pinky putting her list in his hands in a hall full of flowers, which is the house's whole regard for him in one sentence* — and the page says `"Arjun!" She takes the pen out. "Yeh chairs abhi mandap ke paas le jao."` The list never leaves her hand and the order is not his. The strike of a carry and the promise of a regard are the same mistake seen from two sides: a footer may not certify a payment the prose has not made. What the chapter does give him is what the episode gives: the one head that comes up when four men go in at the gate and round the back of the house, and *a girl who has been given nothing will keep that card, and he wants to be there when she opens it, and neither of those is a thought he has a name for yet* — three sentences the `ADDED` row had been describing since 2026-11-11 and the lift had never contained. They are in the lift now, next to `BF-58`, which is unchanged.

**Numbers, from the tools, on the swept file.** Ch8 **683 → 722 l.**, 46,366 → **52,077 B** (`wc -l -c`, measured on the file as published); the scenes' words **6,275 → 6,285** (`camera_check`), ten words net for the whole sitting — three sentences in, four carries out — and the footer grew by what it had to say about it. Book TOTAL **126,862 → 126,872 in 19 chapters** (`recount`). `gap_check` **27/27 = 100%**, 18 music cues in the window, and this is the first chapter of the sweep read into a **full** cover of its episode stretch: the three `gap_check` flags Chapter 7 had were transliteration and paragraph-splitting, and Chapter 8 has none. `picture_check` 14 scenes / 58% / **0 short**; `camera_check` green with N3 at 161 of 275 paragraphs (59%) and narration at 288 sentences, mean 12.7, 47% under nine — *the one figure this chapter was warned about and was not improved on, so the footer says so instead of the tool being quieted*; `thread_check` 14 / 7 / 9 = 64% (1 fenced); `oc_check`, `money_check`, `ripple_check`, `divergence_clock` and `check.py --all` **green**. `OPEN.md` takes the four strikes into `O047` and the `O043` harvest moves **26 → 27** BF rows cited in chapter footers book-wide and **17 → 18** in the chapters read line by line, Chapter 8 having taken up `BF-58`.

## 2026-11-14 — the sweep of Chapter 9 (*The Door That Never Opens*), and the footer rebuilt on the track
**Asked:** keep going, one chapter a sitting, to the workshop's own standard — canon-faithful prose, `O047`/`L-23` clean, and a footer whose every claim is true of the page and re-derived against the caption track rather than the last build's notes.

**Read first, and what the audit notes got wrong.** The whole chapter, and the whole footer, line by line, with the fenced key block stripped before any grep — and the sitting's first finding was that the notes carried down from the audit were wrong about this file. Three defects they named are not on the page at all: there is no truncated reading #1, no *"He is off at the mezzanine… a coil of cable over one shoulder"* passage (that was the *withdrawn* reading, described in the footer and never in the prose), and no second note in pencil on a register's cover with an eye-glass in it — the file has one cover, one line in ink, and a sentence about the track's *"whisper about the kechuli at 37:02"* which is genuinely there, at the tail of the block, whispered by the two women at the pillar. **A remembered page is not a read page.** The rule that keeps biting.

**The five carries the ruling still had in this chapter** (`OPEN.md` `O047`, `foundation/OC_VIHAAN.md` §3b: *He is not the house's helper and not a handyman with a screwdriver, a tray or a pencil* … *He does not carry things. He does not mend things.*). The garland tray at the pillar, which `STAGING` had gone so far as to certify; the crate of the Chapter 4 lobby callback, which Chapter 4 never wrote; the crate of brass in the song, on his forearms; the three cloths off her armful; and one *re-cause* — a porch, a man on his knees, a file, in a sentence about her first visit, all of which was struck out of Chapter 7 in r71. Four paragraphs rewritten, nothing padded in (`L-23`), the neighbours re-read for the clink, `oc_check` and `camera_check` green after.

**The footer, rebuilt as one write on the Chapter 8 template.** Sixteen keys became fifteen; `CORRECTED` created out of a paragraph that had no key line of its own; `ADDED-2` and `FIXED` folded into `ADDED` and `CORRECTED`; `SEEN` put back before `ADDED`; the severed `CHANGED` joined to its own tail; every key re-wrapped at 124 characters with a duplicate-key assert and a word-for-word equality check, so the reflow cannot lose a sentence. Then the claims: **28 cue blocks, 22 with a `[संगीत]` marker, 30 markers** — the score row listed ten and is now all 22, and `gap_check`'s single MISSING (33:00, the ten-beat drum) is the tool's transliteration missing *"Dum dum dum dum."*, footnoted instead of padded; *eleven chapters* became eight, with the note that the man in Chapter 15's basement is **Gurinder**; the *gone from this page* sentence was replaced with what was actually struck; `PICTURE`'s *seven inserts* is now the four that are on the page, and the numbers a tool can still return (8 scenes, mean speech share 38%, 0 short; 6,148 words; 44% N3, under the window, flagged and not repaired). **The song note's citations were to the wrong file** — `§1e` and `§1g` of `STYLE_CANON.md` do not hold the rain, the plate, the chair or the held look; the real owners are `SITUATIONS.md` §1 and §8 and `STYLE_CANON.md` §1e.6, and the vocal-on-contact rule is `SOUND_AND_SCREEN.md` §2 with §9.3, not an invention of this chapter.

**Two things this sitting found in the canon tree itself, and fixed there rather than in the prose.** `canon/SITUATIONS.md` forbids two situations in one scene and schedules the song for Chapter 12 — from the nine-chapter skeleton — while the book's song is in Chapter 9, at the middle of nineteen, with the rain inside it. Both rows amended with the reason and the date, and the exception written as an exception. And this chapter's `ADDED` certified a whole authored scene, `The Service Contract`, with **no row in `BUTTERFLY.md` and no duty in `FORWARD.md`**: the register now has `BF-92`, the ledger has its row, and `FORWARD.md`'s own count sentence — *Eighteen rows… the register holds 83* against a tool printing 25 and 91 — was corrected to match the tool it is supposed to describe.

**Registered, not fixed:** `OPEN.md` `O051`. Chapter 7 sweeps the same glass once already (l.333, l.383) two nights earlier in the book's clock, and no caption carries a broken glass in Episode 3 before 37:46. Rhyme or duplicate is the author's call; neither footer contradicts the other, so nothing was struck.

**Numbers, measured on the swept file.** `wc -l -c` → **685 lines, 55,436 bytes** (was 583 lines, 44,754 bytes), and the footer now runs 15 keys on the Chapter 8 template with nothing over 124 columns. `tools/recount.py`: the book is 126,962 words in the scenes across 19 chapters.
