## 2026-11-14 · The score's file was licensing the errand the OC file forbids — `canon/SOUND_AND_SCREEN.md` §9.2, struck

Chapter 9's sweep struck five places where the page made Vihaan useful — a garland tray at a pillar, a crate in a lobby, a brass crate in a song, three cloths off a woman's armful, a kneeling callback that no page supports. The sweep then went and read the file that tells the composer where his cue plays, and found the reason the beats kept growing back. `canon/SOUND_AND_SCREEN.md` §9.2's *When it plays* row gave the cue three triggers and the second was **when he lifts** — *a crate, a tray, a man's arm out of the way*. `foundation/OC_VIHAAN.md` §3b, the author's ruling of 2026-11-11, runs *He does not carry things. He does not mend things.* One of the two files was going to win, and until today it was not the OC file: a canon row that names an errand as a cue is an instruction to keep writing them, and `RULES.md` `L-23` says a struck errand is not re-caused, so the licence had to go rather than the struck scenes be re-litigated.

The row now carries three positions and no lifting: he **decides**, he **arrives** somewhere he was not expected, he **holds still while a room moves around him** — and it says out loud that the fourth trigger was struck on 2026-11-14, with both filenames in the sentence, so the next reader of §9.2 finds the ruling rather than the gap. The distinction the row now draws is the one Chapter 9's `CORRECTED` key already made about the props: a thing *held* is a man standing somewhere; a thing *carried from one place to another* is an errand. A cue may mark the first and may not mark the second.

**Checked, not assumed:** no tool parses that table. `tools/score_check.py` reads §9 for loyalties and no cue-time, and `check.py`'s score gate and `score_check` on all nineteen chapters were both re-run after the edit — **0 faults** — so nothing was quietly re-tuning itself to the phrase I removed. Two files' worth of text moved: `canon/SOUND_AND_SCREEN.md` one row (181 lines / 20,852 bytes), and `STATUS.md` §38's table one row (inserted with a string splice, which first welded it onto the end of the `FORWARD.md` row above it — two rows on one line, no newline of its own; caught by reading l.932 to l.935 back and split again, since a table row that is not a line is a row no reader and no tool will find) plus the two sentences under it that had been quoting 17-chapter counts since 2026-11-11 (`recount.py` is at 126,962 words in 19 chapters; `franchise_check` prints 12/19 titles, 37/95 body devices, 45/57 omen devices, and those numbers are now what §38 says). This is **r74** and it is a repair build, not a chapter: Chapter 9 is unchanged in it, and the published r73 archive therefore carries a canon line that the workshop no longer does. **r75** then carries what r74 does not: the split of that `STATUS.md` row, this sentence, and the file numbers in `SNAPSHOT.txt`, which r74 reported one HISTORY entry too small because the entry was still being written when the archive was packed. `canon/SOUND_AND_SCREEN.md` §7's stale `screen_check` table is still stale and is still not a source.

## 2026-11-14 · Chapter 10 audited a second time, and seven of the seven faults were in the footer — `chapters/Chapter_10_The_Girl_Nobody_Was_Looking_At_Part_1.md`, rebuilt on the fifteen-key template

**What was asked and what was found.** The chapter was swept once already, on 2026-11-12, and the sweep was believed. This time the five gates that had not been re-run were run first and every one of them passed — `oc_check` green with him seen in three paragraphs, `thread_check` 12 of 17 scenes, `score_check` 0 faults, `money_check` green, `ripple_check` green, `picture_check` 0 of 16 scenes short — and then the footer was read **as raw lines** instead of through a key index, which is where the trouble was. Seven faults, and six of them in the footer's prose; one in the body, and that one opened rather than fixed. **A chapter can pass every gate in the workshop and still be lying in the keys**, because the gates read the scenes and the constraints, not who a sentence belongs to.

**The two that would have propagated.** `EVIDENCE` printed a list of 28 `[संगीत]` times, and the track has 27 in the window: 09:00 was missing and 12:10 and 12:52 were invented — two unmarked speech blocks, one of them the kiss. Anyone re-deriving the cue list from that footer would have scored the chapter against blocks the show does not mark. And `DEEPENED` had been **cut in half by the `REGISTER` block spliced into the middle of it**: its sentence ended *"He comes"* on its own line and resumed five lines later under another key, and its *252 → 473 words* for `The Study Door` had been false for two passes (the scene measures 533 words in 65 paragraphs today). The splice is invisible to every tool and to anyone reading the footer by key name, which is exactly how I read it last time.

**The three that were prose, and what was done about each.** `BF-62` had been registered since the deep pass against a lane scene the chapter had stopped carrying — so the beat went **back into the page** (l.504: the old man with the cut grass, the question dying because a son of that house says the party is inside, the key out of the gravel with the dirt on it still) rather than the claim being deleted, because the register is the promise and the page is the debt (`BF-91`); and the glass of water the promise carried was **refused**, twice over — `OC_VIHAAN.md` §3b ends the carry, and the unread glass is Chapter 9's on this same night at l.182, l.184 and l.358, so a third copy in one evening was repetition dressed as presence. The love theme's *"the same string that came up in the store room two nights ago"* is out of l.312: there is no such cue, the store room is where she puts Dimple's two bags, and Chapter 9's cue is three hours **after** this dance, not before it. **And the envelope at l.580 is struck** — he came round the veranda with it, put it inside his coat, and no chapter in nineteen opens it; it is the same class of carry struck out of Chapter 8, it collided with the unopened envelope three scenes earlier and with `BF-15`, and it was in a canon scene, where nothing of his is allowed to be unaccounted for.

**The one in the body, left standing on purpose.** l.338 says the thing at the base of her spine went up *"the way it went up in the dark of the temple three nights ago"*. The temple is real and findable — `chapters/Chapter_06_The_Kechuli.md` l.668, a temple at midnight, every lamp burning straight, *ye aapki pehli kechuli hai* — but the book's own arithmetic puts that night **one** night before this dance, and Chapter 12's canon line says so in its own words at l.248 (*abhi chaubees ghante bhi nahi hue ise*, at half past one on its own night). **Nothing was moved.** Chapter 12 has not been swept, and a swept chapter must not be repaired to an unswept one; the count is now `OPEN.md` **`O052`**, named in this chapter's `DEFERRED` with the line numbers on both sides of the argument. Last time this sentence was called "half-supported" from a grep of Ch7–Ch9 and that grep was wrong in method, not in luck: it never looked for the temple night itself.

**The tree was where the six stale sentences were, and it went with the chapter.** `foundation/OC_PRESENCE.md` §5 still listed the pillar glass and a six o'clock flight as Chapter 10 touches, and a map file that keeps a struck prop will hand it to the next chapter; `foundation/WORLD_STATE.md`'s Chapter 10 checkpoint called the dance "the first time her skin did something she did not ask it to" when the temple had it first, and stopped the gift-book bullet at "a cheque" although the chapter now turns on a slip that is written twice and taken away by Parmeet; `foundation/BUTTERFLY.md`'s `BF-62` and `BF-63` were amended to what the page carries; `foundation/FORWARD.md`'s `BF-81` row was corrected from *eight lines in Chapter 10* to ten, and from twenty in Chapter 19 to the twenty-two `oc_check` prints today; `canon/episodes.json` still said Episode 4's captions were *"not yet read"* three years of builds after they were read for this chapter, and it has been brought into line (E08 says the same words and is telling the truth, and was left alone); `foundation/RIPPLE.md` §4 and `OPEN.md` `O043` were re-read off the tools, which print 86 untriaged and 92 register rows with 31 cited, not the 78 and 91 and 27 the files were carrying. `Sara/SARA.md` is **not** corrected — its Chapter 10 passages are a dated record of a sitting — and a workshop note was appended after the last entry to say which four sentences it has since outlived.

**Also fixed in the keys, because a footer is a claim about the tools.** `PICTURE` had been publishing the 2026-11-12 visual pass's own report as if it were a measurement of this file — *fifteen of sixteen scenes short*, *twenty-six inserts*, *speech 56% → 49%*, *tenth of eighteen* — where the swept file measures 16 scenes, mean 48%, 0 short, 48 inserts, N3 176 of 338 = 52%, in a book of nineteen chapters. `VOICE` claimed *Get out* and *Mister Suri* as English this chapter speaks; they are B23's and B24's and both land in Chapter 11, so the claim is now stated against those rows with their timecodes. `DEFERRED` handed B26 to Chapter 12 against `canon/E04_BEATS.md`'s own division table, which gives Chapter 11 B18–B26 and Chapter 12 B27–B32. `OC:` was reduced to scene names, because `oc_check` parses that key as a list. `SILENT:` is present and says *none*, with the four scenes that give him no quotation named and the reason on each, so the count is auditable instead of lucky.

**Numbers, measured on the swept file.** `wc -l -c` → **1,089 lines, 87,035 B** (was 871 lines, 61,589 B). The chapter's body is 8,469 words in 17 scenes, and its footer is 15 keys over 336 lines and 6,173 words — **the longest footer in the book, on purpose**, because it carries a nine-item correction record and a cue list re-derived in seconds; `O043` (whether footers should be numbered in bulk) remains the author's call and this build does not pre-empt it. `gap_check --beats` 29 of 29, `score_check` 0 faults in 19, `recount.py` **127,087 words in the scenes, 19 chapters** (up 125 from the lane paragraph and nothing else), `check.py --all` GREEN, `doctor.py` 12 selftests GREEN, the desk at 6.0 MB of 8. Ten files moved: the chapter, five foundation or canon files, `OPEN.md` (a 52nd `O` row, `O052`), `STATUS.md` §38 (eleven rows and both of its summary sentences), `Sara/SARA.md`, and `canon/episodes.json`.

**What this sitting is a warning about, said once and not again.** Every number in a footer that came from a previous build's footer is a copy, and a copy of a count is not a count. The rule that keeps a workshop honest is therefore not *check the tools* — the tools were green on the very build that had six wrong sentences in it — but **re-derive the footer from the files it is talking about, in the sitting that ships it**, and read the footer as lines, because a key can be cut in half by another key and both of them still look like themselves.


## 2026-11-14 · Chapter 11 swept, and the fault that mattered was a window — `chapters/Chapter_11_Mister_Suri.md`, its footer rebuilt; `canon/E04_BEATS.md`, one block added

**The one number that made every other number wrong.** Chapter 11's `BASE` said its window opened at **13:04**, and `gap_check.py` reads the window from `BASE` and from nowhere else — so the chapter had been printing **19/19** on a cue count that included a block belonging to Chapter 10, and the tool could not see it because the tool is *supposed* to trust that line. B17 (13:04–13:56) is Chapter 10's last beat, and Chapter 10's own BASE closes at 13:56 *"so that B18 onward is Chapter 11's"*: the two footers disagreed about one minute of tape, and the ledger's division row had been right the whole time. With the window at 13:56–22:47 the honest number is **18 cues, 17 whole, one PARTLY** — and the PARTLY is disclosed rather than fixed, because the 15:11 block's tail is English written in Devanagari and cut mid-word (*आई एम डन वि*), which is a fact about the caption file, not about this page.

**The cue list, and what a list is for.** `EVIDENCE` printed six `[संगीत]` times and called them the window's. The window holds **fourteen**: 20 blocks, 14 of them carrying a marker, 21 markers because five blocks carry it twice (14:35 ×3, 17:53 ×3, 18:40 ×2, 19:12 ×2, 21:33 ×2), 2 music-only, 6 unmarked speech. Two of the eight it left out are 21:11 and 21:33 — the two blocks this chapter's own reading 6 spends a paragraph on, arguing that the animal is not named there — which is the shape of the whole fault: **the footer was quoting cues in one key that another key denied existing.**

**The key that was not a key.** `REGISTER` had been spliced into the middle of `ADDED`: `ADDED` ended mid-sentence at *while he is on a call*, and its own sentence resumed five lines down inside the next key at *to London, which is the reason he is on that side of the house*, carrying four more of its items with it (the warning in the body, the rescue at the gate, the moon, the vow). This is the third chapter running in which that happened — Chapter 9's `CHANGED`, Chapter 10's `DEEPENED`, Chapter 11's `ADDED` — and in all three the tool suite passed, because `check.py` walks the *beginnings* of keys and both halves looked like keys of their own. The rule that comes out of it is the one this book has been learning all week: **read a footer as raw lines, not through a key index.**

**Five details for an ending that had been cut.** `STAGING` claimed the cabin bag with its strap, the bag put down on the road, the auto that has not come, the compound wall, and a bullet in *not certain* that said the page stops with an auto missing. None of it is in the file. It is in `HISTORY.md` l.787, which is the build that wrote this chapter on 2026-11-11 — *at twenty past eleven, in a room where nobody turns round, the bag set down against the wall — his flight is at six* — and `Sara/SARA.md` l.2378 is titled *the boy who puts his bag down* for it. The visual pass of 2026-11-12 cut the ending and the footer went on describing it for two days. **And that cut line is where Chapter 10's six o'clock came from**: the hour was never invented by a footer, it was copied out of a draft that had already been thrown away, which is why `OC_PRESENCE` §5 now traces it instead of denying it, and why no page of Chapter 10 or 11 names an hour.

**One line put back into the prose.** The 15:11 block — *shaadi wo tumhari behen ke saath. I am done with* — sits inside B19's span and was on no page: the show has Ravish end the affair in one breath, and this book had been giving him three. It is now at l.124, cut where the caption cuts it, and `canon/E04_BEATS.md`'s B19 row logged the block for the first time in the same build, because the ledger had missed it for three years and a chapter cannot cite a cue the ledger does not have.

**The register was more exact than the prose, in three places at once.** *The ruin happens in front of one guest instead of **eight*** was in `BF-19`, in the Chapter 11 checkpoint, and in this footer; the page says *all of them except one* (l.196) and never counts. Likewise *the letter was produced against her **three scenes later*** — in `BF-16`, in the checkpoint, in the footer — when the ask is B13 at 09:44–10:43, which is **the previous chapter**, and the production is this chapter's sixth scene. Both struck in the tree and here, with the line numbers that are true. And `WITNESS` cited `canon/E03_BEATS.md` *B18, B20, B21* for *the pregnancy, the letter's ask, Arjun*: two of the three rows are right, the middle one is the lecture on love, and no gate can catch any of it, because the gate only reads the chapter's own ledger, where B18 to B21 all exist and mean something else. Other episodes' beats are named by title from now on, which is the Ch10 lesson applied one file further along.

**What this chapter had never signed for.** Six rows of `foundation/BUTTERFLY.md` (`BF-16`, `BF-17`, `BF-18`, `BF-19`, `BF-64`, `BF-65`) name Chapter 11 as their landing and the footer cited none of them, so anyone checking whether the promises were paid had to leave the chapter to find out. They are cited now, `BF-17`'s landing in Chapter 16's chemist's slip was re-read and verified there (l.330), and `BF-89` is cited as a **floor** rather than a payment — the book in this chapter is the gift book (l.182, l.382), not the metal-covered one, which no chapter may explain before it is asked for. `FENCE:` fenced `Johro` and left `Ananta's Vow` and `The Account` bare without a word; all three are fenced with the reason on each, and **both** thread figures are published (8/9 = 89% fenced, 8/11 = 73% not), because a fence shrinks a denominator and a reader is entitled to see that it did.

**Chapter 10, one key shorter than the law.** r76 rebuilt Chapter 10 onto the fifteen-key template and took the `OURS:` line out with the old keys — and `foundation/OC_PRESENCE.md` §5's law 2 (`L-24`) is precisely that the non-canon scene be declared on an `OURS:` line. It is restored (l.769: the lane at l.504, the glass's refusal, and the fact that this is the day the key came back), which is the second time this week a repair created a gap by *replacing* a structure instead of *checking* it against the rules that bind it.

**Numbers, measured as they ship.** `chapters/Chapter_11_Mister_Suri.md` **568 → 773 lines, 39,529 → 59,696 B**: body 5,238 words in 12 headings (11 scenes and the precap), `## Footer` at l.475, **17 keys over 300 lines / 5,306 words**, longest line 124 columns. `canon/E04_BEATS.md` one bullet at l.111, 27,704 B. `foundation/OC_PRESENCE.md` §2, §3 and §5 amended and **four** map rows added — the table had stopped at `E03 (Ch 7)`, and Chapter 9's row had been owed since r73; `foundation/BUTTERFLY.md` two rows corrected, 63,497 B; `foundation/WORLD_STATE.md` 56,805 B; `OPEN.md` 80,480 B; `STATUS.md` §38 twelve new rows and both summary sentences, 225,045 B; `chapters/Chapter_10_The_Girl_Nobody_Was_Looking_At_Part_1.md` one key restored, 88,219 B over 16 keys; `canon/STYLE_AUDIT.md` §16 annotated under its own date — its *19/19* and *13:04–22:47* stay because they are the audit's record of the sitting, with the corrected window and counts written beside them. `HISTORY.md` and `Sara/SARA.md` appended rather than rewritten. `recount.py` **127,108 words in the scenes, 19 chapters**.

**The one sentence worth keeping from this build.** Every fault in Chapter 11 was in a file *about* the chapter except the one caption line, and the chapter itself needed one sentence; that is what a footer is for and also what it costs, which is why a footer that is not re-derived in the sitting that ships it becomes a second, better, unfalsifiable copy of the chapter — and then a window that was 52 seconds too wide is true for two days because the only tool that could have seen it takes its order from the footer.

**And the index had the same fault as the footers.** Repairing `SNAPSHOT.txt` for this build found that the r76 build had inserted its own row *inside the preamble sentence that tells the reader which row to read*, because it anchored on a header string that also appears in that sentence. For one whole build, line 34 of the index carried r76's header, its hashes and its `THE BUILD TO READ` claim, and `BUILD r76` had no line of its own. No tool can see it: the index lives outside the workshop and nothing reads it — it surfaced only because this build went looking for where to write, and read the file as raw lines. Split again at r77, with the fact said on the r77 row's own line. **This is the `ADDED` / `REGISTER` fault one level up and it is the same lesson: a splice is invisible to any tool that reads only the beginnings of structures.** Two numbers the index had been told and this build measured were wrong with it — the desk is at **6.30 MB of 8**, not 6.0, and the newest row was at **l.34**, not l.146.

**31:39 is a speech block** (the third-step sentence) and the music there is **31:16 and 31:23**. Both fixed, the

pehen ke jao* and *Aa gayi na, Mummy* among lines "transliterated from the caption track wherever the track carries

**The splice, which is a shape fault the gates cannot see.** `ADDED: The Jacket` ended *it is **his** scene. He*
with eight lines of its continuation sitting under `REGISTER:`, and `CHANGED:` ended at *the vow* with its tail
sitting under a **second `ADDED:`** four keys later. `tools/check.py` reads key *beginnings*, so seventeen keys
were "in order" and green the whole time. The lines were moved back to their own keys, nothing rewritten, and the

**And the fifth fault was the sweep's own.** Before a word was written, this pass quoted Chapter 12's footer at

## 2026-11-14 · Chapter 14 swept, and the footer was more exact than the page — `chapters/Chapter_14_The_Empty_Palm.md`; `OPEN.md` O047; `foundation/BUTTERFLY.md` BF-95

**The find, stated as the reversal it is.** Three footer keys of this chapter described a man repairing a sound panel:
`SEEN:` *on one knee with the panel unscrewed*, `ADDED:` *he does up four of the panel's six screws, and leaves two out*,
`REGISTER:` *the screwdriver finished in front of the man who lied to him*. **The prose contains no panel, no screws and
no screwdriver** — l.1-795 searched, nothing — and what the scene at l.448-464 actually does is the thing
`foundation/OC_VIHAAN.md` §3b asks for: he stands with the vendor's sheet, asks *kisne diya*, is told *Parmeet sir ne*,
turns the sheet over and writes on the back of it, and orders an hour's recordings onto his laptop. The 2026-11-11
rebuild took the mechanic out of the chapter and left his description in the note, and every gate stayed green while it
sat there: `tools/oc_check.py` looks for the *word* for carrying, `tools/check.py` reads key *beginnings*. So the three
keys were re-aligned to the page and **no prose was touched** — the first sweep in this book where the correction ran
from the page to the footer rather than the other way, and the reason `O047` now tells the next chapter to read the
footer **first** and put the prose against it.

**The window, counted off the source.** `EVIDENCE:` printed "`captions/E06.hi.txt` (95 caption blocks)" — the
**file's** count, not the chapter's. The window 00:00:06-22:42 holds **58** blocks: **47** carry `[संगीत]` with **78**
markers between them, and **11** are speech with no marker, **9** of them more than two words. **37** blocks lie after
22:42 — Chapter 15's half, whose own `BASE:` opens exactly at B29's 22:42, so the divide is clean and the count was
only mis-attributed. `tools/gap_check.py` counts the same 47 off `BASE:` and is now named on the page as the number to
trust.

**The six English lines, and the sweep's own mis-count in the same paragraph.** `VOICE:` listed *I remember*, *You
look great*, *I couldn't help it*, *It's okay na*, *Come on, bro*, *I love you, sir* as English "where the speakers speak
it". The first pass on this key searched the track in **Roman** letters, got six nils, and wrote that none of them was on
the track — which is wrong, because a Hindi ASR spells English in Devanagari and a search that does not look for
*यू लुक ग्रेट* is not reading the file. Measured properly, inside 00:00:06-22:42: **05:11** *असिस्टेंट तुम वाओ यू लुक
ग्रेट… आई कुडंट हेल्प इट* (ledger B10, page l.222, l.226); **07:30** and **08:02** *कम ऑन ब्रो* (B14, page l.394, l.486,
l.736); **21:53** *आई लव यू सर* (answering the 18:51 message of B24; page l.634, l.748). Four are caption lines. The
other two are the fault: ***It's okay na*** is on the track only at **35:07** (*इट्स ओके ना?*), which is **B42 — Chapter
15's half** — and the page does not speak it anywhere (l.1-795 searched), so the `VOICE:` key was listing a line from
the other chapter of the same episode; and ***I remember*** has a track neighbour only as Gurinder's *question* at 01:48
(*तुम लोगों को याद है?*, the Attari introduction), while the page gives it to her at l.112 as an **answer** — no ledger
row, no caption, so it is now declared as the workshop's own line. Both corrections are printed inside the key, with the
wrong pass named in it rather than tidied away.

**Two figures, one label.** `PICTURE:` says speech came down "from 60% to 52%"; `camera_check` measures **56%** (speech
in **207 of 368** paragraphs) and `picture_check` reports **19 scenes, mean 51%, 0 short**, so the pass's own report
stands as history with the live figure printed next to it. And "*fourteenth of eighteen*" is now "*fourteenth of
nineteen*", the same stale book-size that `PICTURE:` carried in Chapters 10 and 11. The scene counts themselves are
**three** and none of them is the chapter's fault: 20 `^## ` heads in the body, 19 scenes to `picture_check`, 17 to
`thread_check` with 3 fenced and four scenes printed *bare* — *A Strange Coincidence*, *The Incident*, *Under the Skin*,
*The Hymn, Again*, which are `FENCE:`'s deliberate absences. Recorded on the page; the denominators are `OPEN.md`
O049's tools panel, not this chapter's.

**Nothing was repacked and nothing was uploaded.** Sizes at this writing: chapter **930 l / 59,045 B** (892 / 54,208 in r77), `OPEN.md` **259 / 83,166**, `foundation/BUTTERFLY.md` **252 / 68,253**; `STATUS.md` and this file are written after this row and their final sizes are in the `LIVE, NOT PACKED` row of `SNAPSHOT.txt`, which is measured last on purpose. ## 2026-11-14 · Chapter 15 swept, and the footer was corrupted in a way no gate can see — `chapters/Chapter_15_Two_Weddings_One_Day.md`; `OPEN.md` O047, O049; `foundation/BUTTERFLY.md` BF-96

**The mechanical one first, because it is new.** This chapter's footer carried a block an earlier patch had written in
with its escapes **unconverted** - two literal `\u2019` - a stray `"` at the head of the line, four-space continuations
where the file indents continuations by fourteen, and the word `FIXED:` inside a line that was therefore **not a key**.
It sat between `ADDED-3` and `ADDED-2` as an orphaned note while the real `FIXED:` stayed four keys lower, and every
gate in the workshop called the tree green, because `check.py` counts key *beginnings* and this line did not begin like
a key. Repaired by hand - escapes converted, indentation normalised, the note folded into `FIXED:` as its second entry,
both entries then probed for presence - and the proposal for the tools is written into `O049`: no `\uXXXX` in a shipped
file, no malformed key line, and a key count that has to match the template the footer is built on. The repair took
three attempts: two of my own scripts were interrupted before writing, and one slice arithmetic dropped a line, which
is why this row records that the file was **restored from `/tmp/patch-backup/` twice** rather than patched on top of
itself.

**Then the substantive one, which is Chapter 14's fault with the signs reversed.** `SEEN:` claimed *a pole in his
hands* on the lawn at two in the morning and *a crate of marigold against his chest* at the wedding. On the page the
coil of cable is on the **workman's** shoulder (l.232, l.244, l.260), Vihaan holds *the light-and-sound vendor's final
bill in one hand and his phone in the other* (l.230, and again at l.250 and l.264), and the morning scene (l.606-612)
has no crate in it at all. **The third image is real** - the folder down two floors (l.356), the carton carried back up
(l.378), held at the print desk (l.392) - so this chapter keeps it and carries the §3b question the way Chapter 12
does: both sides printed in `ADDED` 1, no prose touched, `OPEN.md` O047 now holding one question for two chapters,
where a yes costs nothing and a no costs two scenes their reason for being in the room.

**The window, and the arithmetic that looks wrong on purpose.** 95 blocks is the **episode**; 22:42-36:24 holds **38**,
of which **23** carry `[संगीत]` with **36** markers between them, and **15** are speech blocks with **13** over two
words; `tools/gap_check.py` counts the same 23 off `BASE:`. Chapter 14's window holds 58. 58 + 38 = 96 = 95 + 1: the
**block at 22:42 is counted by both chapters**, because the ledger's own cut straddles it (B28 21:53-22:42, B29
22:42-23:15). Both pages say so now, because two honest half-counts read as a double-read track until somebody writes
the boundary down.

**A key checked and found sound, which earns its own paragraph.** `VOICE:` lists five English lines and claimed no
provenance for them. Tested in **Devanagari** - the only way this file can be read, since a Hindi ASR spells English
as sound and a Roman search returns nils on a track that carries the words, which is exactly how Chapter 14's sweep
got it wrong that morning - all five are inside this window: *I don't care* 34:33, *It's okay na* and *You come with
me* in one block at **35:07**, *Don't mind* 35:07 and 35:45, *All set* 32:46, against the page at l.562, l.634, l.640,
l.646 and l.612. The *okay na* line Chapter 14 had to strike for belonging across the divide **belongs here**: 35:07 is
B42 and B42 is Chapter 15's. One gap is the ledger's and not the chapter's: *All set* is on the track and in no row of
`canon/E06_BEATS.md`.

**Counts and claims.** `PICTURE:` said *fifteenth of eighteen* - the book has nineteen - and *speech comes down from
61% to 54%*; `camera_check` measures **58%** (speech in 186 of 321 paragraphs) and `picture_check` reports **12 scenes,
mean 53%, 0 short**, so the ten short scenes are closed history and the pass's own 54% stands beside the live figure.
The body carries 13 `^## ` heads, `picture_check` counts 12 scenes, `thread_check` counts 8 with **5 fenced** - *The
Sisters' Room*, *The Cake*, *The Basement*, *The Storm*, *Twelve Hours*, which are `FENCE:`'s five names exactly - and
that is three measurements, published as three, not a fault in the chapter. `oc_check` green on 23 places;
`score_check` green; `RIPPLED:` carries its reasons now, with R-01 the one that bites: the sisters' scene keeps the
show's beat and **substitutes** the referent, so the name in Ananta's mouth is Vihaan's and Aryaman is in no mouth here.

**No build, on the standing instruction.** `chapters/Chapter_15_Two_Weddings_One_Day.md` is now **838 l / 54,565 B**
against **796 / 49,097** at r77; the body's word count is untouched at **6,516**, which is why the tree `recount` still

## 2026-11-14 · Chapter 16 swept, and the fault that no key-level check can see — `chapters/Chapter_16_The_Warrant.md`; `OPEN.md` O047, O049; `foundation/BUTTERFLY.md` BF-97

**A chapter can lie to itself in two keys and stay green.** `ADDED` 1 declared, as one of the chapter's four authored touches,
that he writes *the four cars' numbers* in the guard's book and that *the book becomes evidence of a morning the family will
spend the afternoon denying, and it goes out of the house with him*. Two pages down, this chapter's own `FIXED:` — the key that
struck out a duplicate order — ends by saying the numbers and the white car *have moved back to where they belong: the gate, in
the morning, in the guard's book*. **Both cannot be true**, and the prose is not on `ADDED` 1's side: he writes **one** number,
the white car's, with the time under it and the men he can count inside it (l.408, once already at l.100), the guard has
written three names himself (l.406), and the book never leaves the flat of the wall where Shyam keeps it (l.78, l.104, l.398).
`check.py` reads keys; `gap_check` reads cues against the track; `oc_check` reads presence. Nothing in the workshop reads **one
key against another**, and that is `BF-97` and a new item in `OPEN.md` O049.

**Chapter 16's prose is clean, and that is the useful part.** After Chapter 14's invented sound panel and Chapter 15's pole and
crate, here the sound table is **real** — *on his knees at the sound table with a cable in his hand* (l.60), the coil tucked
under it with his foot, *Paanch baje se* (l.68) — the borrowed microphone is real (l.72), the count of four cars is real
(l.86, l.108), *Ji* to canon's order is real (l.164-168), the refusal sentence is real (l.352), the white car following one car
back out of an open gate is real (l.416, l.434, l.438), and so is the shoe on the wrong foot (l.420-428). What was wrong is
only what the footer **added**: the book under his arm, the books carried out of the house in a carton — the carton comes *in*
(l.304), his father tells him to put it away *inside* (l.388), he says *Ji* (l.390), and what leaves with him is his own folder
(l.420, l.497) — a phone *ringing since eight* (the page says *subah se*, l.136; *since eight* is the flower man's tempo in the
lane, l.86), and one `STAGING:` still-life, *the red light at the corner*, which the chapter does not contain. **A footer can
manufacture a §3b breach the chapter never commits**: `O047` now runs Chapters 12, 14, 15 and 16, and the reading order is
footer first precisely because the prose has been innocent in three of the four.

**The citation that outlived its scene.** `FIXED:` argued that canon's order had *already* sent him with the girls *at
twenty-three minutes to one*. That time is not in this chapter — the book's clock has the gate at ten (l.78), the hall at half
past ten (l.224), the hotel at ten past twelve (l.443) and The Bargain at a quarter to one (l.583) — and *twenty-three minutes
to one* was the **cut** car scene's own timecode. A struck scene can leave a live citation in a key, and the key then reads as
proof because it is arguing about a scene the footer itself deleted. Canon's order is B05 (02:32-03:04). `O049` takes a fifth
item for it: a clock or timecode named inside a footer key must occur in the body that key describes.

**`VOICE:`, tested in Devanagari, and one line that did not exist.** *What a relief, Dad* and *Finally* are **canon's own
English** — `canon/E07_BEATS.md` B13 prints Ravish saying them at 08:11-13:31 and the chapter gives them at l.529. *I'll tell
you* (l.268) and *That is not necessary* (l.288) are **the chapter's**: the track's only ज़रूरत anywhere in 70 blocks is at
02:32 and it is Parmeet's canon line *Uska koi zaroorat nahi hai* (l.160), a different speaker refusing a different request. **What the test proves and what it does not:** a Latin-letter nil on a Hindi ASR file is
a script artifact, so each line was searched in Devanagari as well, and the nil for *I'll tell you* means **no rendering was found in this
file by these searches** - not that the episode's audio cannot be heard saying the same thing in Hindi. The claim on both pages is therefore
the weaker, checkable one: the English is the chapter's own, sitting on B08's cue.
**Struck by the sweep: `*Inspector good boy*` occurs nowhere in this chapter**, and before it was written the file held that
string exactly once — in the `VOICE:` key quoting itself. Two more lines are now labelled so nobody reads them back as the episode's:
*Wah* (l.62) and *Paanch baje se* (l.68) have no वाह and no पाँच बजे on the track, which is fine, because `STAGING:` declares
the sound table authored; the fault would be the silence.

**The window, and the same straddle as Episode 6.** `captions/E07.hi.txt` is 70 blocks running 00:00:12 to 29:14; this
chapter's window (00:00:12-13:31) holds **30**, of which **18** carry `[संगीत]` with **25** markers and **12** are speech
blocks, every one of them longer than two words; `tools/gap_check.py` reads the same 18 and lands **26/26 cues**, 25 whole and

13:31 is counted by both chapters**, because the ledger's cut between B12 and B13 sits on top of it. Chapter 16's page says so
now; Chapter 17's will when it is swept.

**Counts, and the caveat this chapter gave the tooling.** `PICTURE:` said *sixteenth of **eighteen*** in a book of nineteen —
corrected — and *Sixteen of seventeen scenes were short*, which was true of the pass and is closed history: `picture_check`

ways — written down rather than reconciled, because the pass's *Twenty-four inserts and paragraphs* cannot be audited without
knowing which set counted them (`picture_check`'s `inserts` column now sums to 44). Mechanically: **`WITHDRAWN:` was used

## 2026-11-14 · Chapter 17 swept, and a footer turned out to be lagging its own corrections — `chapters/Chapter_17_The_Reed_and_the_Brass_Knife.md`; `OPEN.md` O047, O049; `foundation/BUTTERFLY.md` BF-38 (corrected), BF-98

**Two keys ended on objects that are not in the chapter, and a third key on the same page had already said so.** `SEEN:` finished
*going down to the basement through the fifth button to find the crates gone and a chewed chinagraph pencil on the floor*; `ADDED` 3 finished
*he puts the pencil in the guard's book with the bent gold pin, and shuts it*. The body contains **no pencil** and **no pin** but the
inspector's lost badge pin (l.214), and the gate's book is two hundred lines and six hours away, wet at the corner of the day's page (l.286).
Twenty lines below both, `THE BUSINESS` reads: **"*The basement is photographed, not collected:* the chinagraph pencil and the bent gold pin of
the earlier draft are gone."** The page's real ending is better than the draft's — camera out of its case (l.646), four angles and two close-ups,
the flash putting his own shadow on all four walls (l.648-650), the card into his shirt pocket (l.652) and into the drawer with the *bina
vivaran* folder and the file with no name on it (l.662) — and it had been there since the 2026-11-12 pass. **A footer can lag a fix recorded in
its own key**, and no tool compares a key with a later key: that is `BF-98`, on top of Chapter 16's `BF-97`, and `O049` now has item (g): an
object named in a footer key must occur in the body that key describes.

**And the pencil is banned by name.** `foundation/OC_VIHAAN.md` §3b: *he is not the house's helper and not a handyman with **a screwdriver, a
tray or a pencil**.* So the invented-object fault and the carrying fault are the same fault seen from two ends, and the count now runs one prop
per chapter — Chapter 14's screwdriver (invented, struck), Chapter 12's tray (real, open in `O047`), Chapter 17's pencil (invented, struck).
**Two of the three were never in the book at all**, which is the whole argument for reading **footer first** in `O047`'s pass of Chapters 9 to
19. Chapter 17 is also the chapter §3b's own paragraph names — *the warrant at his gate takes the morning from him… he gets it back by the

what he holds**, and `Aapko content chahiye, mujhe signature` is on the page and not only in the footer.

**Calls counted from rings.** `SEEN:` and `ADDED` 2 both said *three unanswered calls to his own gate and a fourth where somebody picks up and
says nothing*. The page is two calls: his own house, four rings, nothing; Shyam's phone, answered on the **third** ring by a silence before the
line dies (l.422-428). The footer had read the page's two **ring** counts as a number of **calls** — the same species of mis-add that made

photographed, is the proof that the ledger knew and the footer lagged.

**`VOICE:`, and the limits of an ASR.** *What the hell* and *sau percent* occur nowhere in this chapter — the *footer quoting itself* pattern now
named on every unswept page — and are struck. *Trust me* (l.334) is on the track at **23:24**; *Finally* (l.236) at **20:39**; *I said, naagmani
de* (l.246) sits on the नागमणि at **17:42** and **20:39**. *Finally* also stands in **Chapter 16's** list, and that is not a double-count: the
episode says the word twice, 08:11 (B13, Ravish) and 20:39 (here). What the sweep says it cannot prove is on the page too: a Latin-letter nil on
a Hindi ASR file is a script artifact, so a nil means *no rendering was found in this file*, not *the audio does not say it*.

## 2026-11-14 · Chapter 18 swept, and the footer turned out to be broken in a way no gate can see — `chapters/Chapter_18_The_Faces_in_Her_Eyes.md`; `OPEN.md` O047, O049 (i); `foundation/BUTTERFLY.md` BF-42 (corrected), BF-45 (precised), BF-99

**What was asked, and what it found.** The standing order is chapter by chapter: read the page against `captions/E08.hi.txt` and `canon/E08_BEATS.md`, fix what can be fixed, register. Chapter 18 is `The Faces in Her Eyes`, E08 whole — the window 00:00:09 to 00:31:15, beats B01 to B21, the episode with the buried bride, the thirteen faces in the mud, the van into the river, the PSA, the two thousand cards, the sell-everything list and the yard with the crocodiles. **The prose passed. The footer did not exist as a footer.**

**The seventh shape.** Chapter 11 and Chapter 9 were caught with a key cut in half; here it happened and **nothing said so**. `STAGING:` stopped mid-sentence at *the necklace said*, and its remaining lines sat **below the `RIPPLED:` block**, one of them beginning at column 0 — markdown reads that as a new paragraph, and `tools/check.py` reads a key's *beginning* and never its end, so `--all` printed GREEN over a footer whose keys were prose. `CHANGED`, `Added`, `DEFERRED` and `Seen` were `**BOLD:**` sentences: the substring test is satisfied by `**CHANGED:**`, while every name-based reader misses them — `gap_check` parses `BASE:`, `oc_check` parses `OC:`, and a paragraph is invisible to both. `VOICE:` and `PICTURE:` had never been written for this chapter at all. `RIPPLED:` said `none` with no reasons. The rebuild is `## Footer` onward, fifteen keys in house order, each one extracted from the old text and asserted before it was written; the tail's `BF-88` money sentence became its own `MONEY:` key.

**What the content keys were wrong about.** `**Canon.**` quoted *"humein mahadev wapas chahiye"* as the kul's demand: **0 hits in `canon/E08_BEATS.md`, 0 `वापस चाहिए` on the track**; B04 says *"Aapko humari Maha Naagrani ko wapas laana hi hoga… aapko unhein lautana hi hoga"* and that is what the footer quotes now. `SEEN:` had *the family watching its own advertisement on the wall monitor with the sound low*; the page has the **channel's floor** at a **big screen** with the **sound off** (l.230-236). It had *four phones in four rooms at the same second*; the same line lands on phone after phone in **one** dark hall, over a tray of four glasses (l.508-514). `STAGING:` and `CHANGED:` both called the object a **necklace**; the page's word is **set** — *"Pinky, card chahiye to apne gale ka set bech de. Woh sola hazaar gram hai. Chaar lakh mil jaayenge"* (l.440) — and eleven lines later Aryaman says *"Woh set mat bechna. Main paisa de doonga"* (l.452), which is the one time in the book so far that a brother overrules another brother's money in front of the family. That pair went into `MONEY:` because it is the ₹40 lakh doing three jobs in a fortnight, exactly as `foundation/OC_VIHAAN.md` §3b reckons.

**The finding that was wrong, recorded rather than removed.** My first pass charged §3b with citing this chapter for a line the chapter "does not contain", and wrote it into three files before I checked. **The line is at l.440.** The search had used *sola hazaar grams*, with an s, against a page that says *gram*, and the author's ellipsis spans words rather than replacing them. So §3b is sound, its Ch19 half (*"Chalis lakh, tau ji. Ek bigha nadi ki taraf."*, l.328) is sound, the charge is retracted on the chapter's `DEFERRED:` and in `O047`, and `BF-42` was corrected in place (the butterfly names an object the page never uses) with `BF-45` precise-ised (the report is *reported over the phone to Verma at three*, not *filed at three*). `BF-98`'s lesson was that a truncated line manufactures a false absence; this chapter makes it a rule: **print the whole line before you claim an absence**, and publish the retraction in the same breath as the remaining findings.

**Windows, measured.** `gap_check` reads **47 of 49** cues with **54** music cues in the window — the first non-clean coverage in the series, and both exceptions are the author's and both check out: 00:10:14 is the रग रग नग cue this table scene is built around, and 00:21:32 is the track's *लाइव स्टू? आर यू रेडी। एक और भी बैक में। यू वांट से इट?*, which is why the page has one English line and why `VOICE:` quotes it with its line number (l.320). It is written as a **confirmed** gap, not a repaired one. Caption file: 89 blocks, 54 music, 76 markers, 35 speech, 21 over two words. `picture_check` **13 scenes / mean 59% / 1 short**, all thirteen opening on their place, the short being `## Manohar` at 36 paragraphs and 67% speech with only 2 inserts — **registered, not silently patched**, because closing it needs one or two new insert paragraphs and new prose is not a sweep's to write. `camera_check` **60%** (speech in 171 of 285 paragraphs), `thread_check` 14 scenes / named in 8 / carried in 9 (64%), `oc_check` declared in 7 scenes and on the page in 40 places, `score_check` green on this track. `recount` **7,434** scene words; `camera_check` says 7,433 — one word apart on different units, and both are quoted because both get read. The body has **14** `^## ` heads; the tools count **13** scenes because `## What Comes Next` is the precap.
