## 59 · Episode 10: correction against the dialogue and the beat ledger

The first ten episodes were checked against the available caption tracks and workspace beat notes, not watched. The streaming site could not be played in this environment. A keyword/transliteration tool can suggest where dialogue appears; its percentages do not verify speakers, staging or complete fidelity.

Chapter 20 was revised: the dance-floor encounter belongs to Aryaman, and Aryaman warns his friend Vikram, who shows him the photograph; the Naagmani and crocodile exchange is restored without assigning uncertain speakers; Swarna and Ravish's exchange follows the track; and Sameer's possessiveness is restored. The murder and arrest are at Sameer's house, while the Suri family learn of them via news. The jail confrontation belongs in jail, not at the murder. Vihaan no longer overhears the murder or identifies Ahana from her voice.

A prior draft of this note and the chapter contained serious mistaken additions, now removed: the E09/E10 recap line about an easy death was wrongly assigned to Mahasapera instead of Ahana; invented lines about something being "ajeeb" were presented as track dialogue; Ravish and Vikram were each wrongly treated as a blood relative of the other party in a scene; and unsupported identities were given to the speakers in the four-men exchange. The E09 37:09 track establishes the context of the repeated opening. These were not evidence of a video watch.

The earlier expanded Vihaan-at-the-murder sequence and related material are preserved in `revisions/Chapter_20_before_location_repair.txt`, not in the chapter. Existing adaptation and forward notes referring to that sequence need review. The chapter declares one OC scene; `oc_check.py` still requires at least two and therefore reports RED, a conflict with the stated one-scene instruction rather than proof that the new chapter is wrong. Beat B27 in the ledger and B10's flashback remain open for verification, as does the four-men roster. Chapter 20 remains a draft.

## 62 · 2026-11-14 — E10 evidence sources reconciled; Chapter 20 not revised

The review cross-read the 37:32 caption text with the two locally held E10 written updates and the current season digest, beat ledger, adaptation notes and rulings. **No episode video or audio was watched.** Captions remain dialogue text without speaker labels and omit silent action; TellyReviews/TellyBoosters remain written-update claims, not visual proof.

- E10 00:19 reprises the E09 “easy death” exchange; it is not evidence about Ahana's intent toward Aryaman. At 00:40 the assistant says the guru forgot his been; the current chapter's cave blocking is not settled by that caption.
- The 08:36 dialogue supports the flirting words, while the bump/catch is reported by the updates. The author's correction is preserved: Ahana does **not** target Aryaman; keep the neutral bump line. At 10:08 the photo is discussed in dialogue, but TellyReviews' “trapped him / her plan” wording is recap interpretation, not settled intent.
- B10's past-dance flashback is reported by both held updates but is not established by the caption track. It remains a recap-sourced silent visual, unverified without video.
- B14/O058 is left open: two updates report two cameras out; the caption mentions a camera and a later bulletin reports CCTV footage. The source record does not identify a device or prove what it recorded. BF-114 now labels its three-day camera outage as an adaptation premise rather than episode evidence.
- The 27:15 cue is Chapter 20's single unmatched cue. It is an unlabeled, noisy plea containing “please,” “I love you” and “I die without you”; Sameer is plausible from context and recaps, not confirmed by a speaker label. It is a candidate omitted line, not auto-inserted.
- B27's “want to know who I am / listen carefully” line is in TellyReviews' recap and related TellyBoosters precap material, not in the available caption track through 37:32. It remains unverified as E10 dialogue.

`canon/E10_BEATS.md`, `canon/SOURCES.md` and E10 in `canon/SEASON.md` now distinguish captions from recap claims. `OPEN.md` O058 is narrowed to the unresolved camera setup; O032 controls the knowledge ruling (Parmeet does not know; nobody in the Suri house knows), the contradictory duplicate “Parmeet knows” block was removed, and the unrelated father-face question was renumbered OPEN-20. The stale Chapter 20 placement in `foundation/ORIGINAL_SCENE.md` was corrected; BF-114 was qualified as adaptation.

**Chapter 20 was not edited.** Its pre-review SHA-256 was `929059d19382467dad5b26858171eb6f355fd00c881b837c2e2f9f192f934410`. Remaining for the later prose pass: decide whether to adapt the recap-only B10 flashback; review rather than auto-add the 27:15 line; avoid asserting a specific camera/recording path; reconcile the cave's forgotten-been dialogue with its staging; keep B27 out unless a source changes; and do not add an OC scene just to satisfy `oc_check.py`'s conflicting two-scene floor. The four-men roster at 13:03 remains unassigned. The evidence review is complete to the material held in the workspace; full visual verification remains unavailable.

## 63 · B10 source distinction and O058 footer follow-up

**2026-11-14.** A source-summary pass corrected B10's specificity. TellyReviews describes a flashback in which memories of Ahana dancing with Aryaman in the past resurface. TellyBoosters says her gaze follows Aryaman and triggers memories of past moments, without naming a dance or the same flashback. These are recap reports with different specificity, not two confirmations of one identical image; the captions do not establish the silent sequence, and no video was watched. The author refused another patch (*\"I want you to solve this problems from roots\"*), and he was right to: the fault has now been flagged six times (WRITING.md §0's count), and every repair so far left the machine that produces it untouched. Three roots were dug out and each got a structural fix.

## §88 — No putting Vihaan in at the end; Ch23 re-derived Twin-style (2026-11-18)

The author's ruling, verbatim in sense: he had flagged before that this work stages canon by copy-paste and puts Vihaan in at the end; the §86 repair itself was that pattern. Copy-pasting canon does not work. A butterfly can be a word's — big, small, and all things. He ordered the complete solution learned from the Dragon Ball Z Twin.

## §99 — He never hated her (O070); the embrace designed; room/after-marriage audit (2026-11-19)

Three rulings in one breath. First: Vihaan never hated her — not Purvi, not Ahana, not any face of hers; he does not tell her so, he has too many questions for her, and he goes soft looking into her eyes. Registered as O070, and canon's B19 hate exchange re-derived on it: the track's merged block (00:26:02, Devanagari, speakers unlabeled) lets the hate stay hers, and he returns none — eyes, softening, questions held unasked, the file's close for an exit. The audit confirms this was the only page in nineteen chapters putting hate for her in him (Ch21's *nafrat-better* line is Aryaman's to Pinky; Ch19's *sirf nafrat* is her claim, and now rhymes). VOICE, STAGING and R-02 signed in both halves.

Second: the morning-after embrace (O069/BF-119) designed in canon style, per O027/O028, SHESHVEER §2 and SUPERNATURAL's four rules. It is capacity without knowledge: in sleep his body does what it knows (the arm finds her, his breathing slows hers, he shields) while nothing in him wakes to claim it. For him the onset's first stirring passes through sleep instead of breaking him — rest, never knowledge (O022). For her, fresh from kechuli night, the moon-spent body stands down: the shed-night's tenderness settles and she sleeps through — the first warmth neither chooses. The world reacts before they do and nobody explains it (the lamp steadies, the wind stills, the moon keeps watch); no love named, no recognition, no memory. Limits: it helps the night, never the plot (O068's trap stands); never a cure, transfer, or lecture; a standing pattern from the first morning. Open: which room (his stages the chasma, hers reckons with Ch23's powder line); who finds them.

Third: the room/after-marriage audit the author demanded. On the records nothing is dropped: his room's wealth-fragments staged in Ch2 (§47/BF-110); the chasma owed its arrival and parked its may-discovery (O066); his door/window/lamp staged in Ch24's seam; the shared room and embrace owed E14 (O069/BF-119, now designed); O055's four costs, Min-ji-from-the-money (BF-113), the trap (O068), and the onset's progression all owed forward, none due yet. If the author named a room-thing outside this list, it is not in the records and he should name it again.

## §101 — The loosening plays canon-big, not restrained-small (2026-11-19)

The author struck the staging law the workshop had just written for O071 (*never explained, signs small and bodily, each costing somebody, the world reacting first*) and ordered canon style. He was right: that law was the house's restrained minimalism (`SUPERNATURAL.md`), misapplied to divine machinery. Canon plays Mahadev-scale law operatic, and O071 now says so: the loosening stages in the franchise's order (the body's warning, the eyes, the sound, the skin, the form, the cost — at least three steps, in that order, 1h.1), the hold before the strike, the household as chorus and the deity as given with no irony about faith, fate or the kul. What may be told is told aloud by elder or god mouths — declarative, repetitive, public, never hedged — while narration never lectures. The price stays canon (every easing costs somebody, spoken aloud before it is paid, §10.4/S-17), every large turn traces to a decision — the god's, the kul's, the pearl's — never coincidence (§11), and canon's own restraint holds where canon holds it: the form undescribed until canon describes it. What stays shut is only the old life (SHESHVEER §3): the curse is this season's law and may be spoken; Sheshveer is not. The signs play big around the pair while neither connects them to a curse lifting. BF-120 and the FORWARD row reworded to match; `SUPERNATURAL.md` itself stands for human-scale scenes.

## §107 — The temple re-derived: law, not blood; two corruptions repaired (2026-11-19)

The author read the temple half of Chapter 25 and wept — and named four faults, all mine. (1) Bharani asks *Zinda kyun chhoda?* of Aryaman: in this serial he is a known innocent and she knows it, so the death-question is incoherent. (2) Bharani's elbow-press lays cruelty on him (*usne jab taras nahin khaya*) and orders his death: he did nothing cruel, and the dhokha was the Ep7 stolen face, never his act. (3) The precedents name Shivanya for loving a human: her partner was Sheshveer, a naag. (4) The whole spine fights the chapter's own ending — the middle demands a death the Smriti scene already answers with guard-and-wipe. The pattern charge stands: canon's reflexes pasted over our truths, against `O041` (2026-11-12: nothing is copied until the butterfly ledger has been run over it).

Canon's ground, re-read for the repair: B09's confession response, kill-press and massacre recall; B10's denial, Aryaman-named dhokha, kill-press and dagger (TOI single-witness) stopped by love-for-him; B11's law, four precedents and enmity-teeth (*jaani dushman*); B12's hanging begun, counter-order, book-paradox (the same book gave reason to kill and reason not to) and plea; B13's memory-vidya order, refusal, tapasya-price, blue herb and posted rakshaks. TR/TB confirm canon's Bharani insists he must die — canon's geometry, void here.

The re-derivation, premise-level (§106's method): the dagger is the kul-law's *sentence*, never anyone's hatred. Bharani speaks only the cited sentence (*dand/niyam*), reluctantly, steeling the queen with the house's true plural wound; she never asks why he lives and never lays cruelty on him. Teeth are his aired resolve to *speak*, never enmity. What stays Ahana's hand is innocence-known (*The man under the dagger never held the knife*); the TRUE Purvi-Vihaan memories anchor who she is. The aadesh overrules the law's letter, the kitab's paradox reads literal (death is the sentence, breath the stay), and B13 vindicates her without a word changed. Precedents minus Shivanya per new `O072` (entailed already by `O022`'s Season-1-female-lead clause; now name-level and unmissable); Bela, Shesha, Vani stand as aired, unruled. Canon's shape — confession, press, attack, ultimatum, hanging, book, plea, stay — is fully preserved; every content-word of it is now ours.

Corruption found under the work: `canon/E14_BEATS.md` carried a literal `...[truncated 3881 chars]` where B11's tail through §2 had been (a truncated read re-saved as the file), and Chapter 25's own tail carried `...[truncated 2119 chars]`. Both tails rebuilt — the beats from a whole re-read of captions 00:20:16–00:27:35 with TR/TB/KB spot-held and TOI confirmed ending at B10 (full recap re-verification marked pending where missing), the footer with a `FIXED:` record; the §104 register rows verified alive in `foundation/BUTTERFLY.md` + `foundation/FORWARD.md`. Lesson, sharp enough to be law (L-40): never write a file from a displayed read — bytes in, bytes out, `tail` verified.

## §108 — The author's second temple complaint: the love-mechanism leak + butterfly law becomes code (2026-11-19)

The author returned with the v1 lines quoted back — *Zinda kyun chhoda?*, *Maar-iye ise*, Shivanya among the precedents, the elbow-press — and this time the charge included a sentence the v2 repair had genuinely missed: *Moments when she loved — him ... as Purvi, before the oath buried it.* Accounting, verified by grep on the current bytes: the four §107 faults are gone from the file (the confession is answered with resolve-not-death at l.321, the press speaks only *dand/niyam* at l.363, the law carries no name at l.381, precedents read *Bela. Shesha. Vani.* at l.383) — the quoted v1 words match the pre-repair draft, not the presented file. The love sentence was live at l.367 and false on two counts: she never loved Aryaman (his question is unanswerable — no such beat exists anywhere in the workshop), and it contradicted the repair's own premise four lines down (*The man under the dagger never held the knife*, l.371). It was canon's aired mechanism (TOI/B10: love stays her hand) carried into v1; the repair added the new thesis beside it instead of replacing it. Replaced now with the acquittal frame (*Moments that acquit him — every one of them Purvi's, from before the oath buried her*); rescan identical to baseline (A=0/B=0/C-new=19 queued for the sweep), `butterfly_check` green on all 40 chapter files.

Root cause, as the author demanded it plainly: (1) the pipeline optimises track-fidelity — `STYLE_CANON.md` says carry the track's Hindi, the draft step runs evidence→beats→chapter, and butterfly rulings applied at repair-time, so every first draft is born violating; (2) every mechanical gate tested the track and none tested our canon — `O041` (his own 2026-11-12 core-problem ruling) and `O072` stayed prose, executable by nobody; (3) repair-by-addition, proven by l.367; (4) franchise reflex — aired/TOI stop the dagger with love, and the hand carried the mechanism while changing the words. Same family as the Sheshveer-name error: canon's reflexes pasted over our truths.

Structural fix, all in this section: `tools/butterfly_check.py` (`O072` name ban, §107 death-question/cruelty/death-order patterns Aryaman-scoped, §108 love-falsehood patterns; prose-only so footers stay free to discuss rulings; Ch22's inspector-context line verified passing), wired into `tools/check.py --all`; `RULES.md` L-41 (butterfly-first drafting + repair-by-replacement); this record. ## §111 — The author rules the dharma-line: a naagrani cannot kill an innocent (2026-11-19)

He quoted v3's sentence-language back (*Nirnay sunana hi hoga*) and asked if this workshop is crazy: Ahana knows Aryaman is innocent — she knows he was not present that night — and a naagrani cannot kill an innocent; Naagin 3 is the proof, where the villains' whole game was to make Bela kill innocent Mahir so they could take the naagmani. He added the face-fact: in our story Bharani took Vihaan's form that night, not Aryaman's like canon. Two admissions are owed on the record. First, the face-fact was already established in this workshop (OPEN.md E10-continuity: Bharani wore Vihaan's face while the real Vihaan was away in Ghaziabad; Ahana unlearned) — v4's research described it as *someone* and never surfaced the wearer; the author should never have had to re-teach a filed truth. Second, v1–v4 all kept death on the table in some form (sentence, hanging-as-containment, death written by the kitab) — every one of those readings is now void, because innocent blood on a naagrani's hands unmakes the queen, which is exactly why the N3 villains wanted it.

v5 removes the sentence-frame root and branch: L363 presses an answer, not a nirnay (*chup na rahiye*); L385 states the Bela-law in Bharani's own mouth (innocent blood unmakes the queen — *Bela se Mahir ka khoon karwaana chaha tha unhone, naagmani ke laalach mein*) with the burden delimited (*rokne ka, baandhne ka, maarne ka nahin*); L393–399 raise a binding, not a hanging (feet stopped, never breath; *bandhan kholo*); L401's kitab writes forgetting, not death — the wipe ordained. L215's death-line stands untouched: it is spoken to Rishabh, a guilty killer, not an innocent. No death of Aryaman stands anywhere in B09–B13, by any hand. Canon layer: RULES.md L-42 (the dharma-law); E14_BEATS §2 dharma-boundary bullet; OPEN.md face-bullet carried current through Ch25; `butterfly_check.py` S111 (Aryaman-explicit kill-strings, noose-words with Ch21's joke allowlisted at l.343, hanging-residue, `hang(s) in the air` Aryaman-windowed so Ch19's snake passes).

## §122 — The mark and the showing: Anantveer named again, faceless, at the author's word (2026-11-19)

The author's lore ruling: Vihaan's back bears the five-hooded golden naag — in the Naagin universe the mark the ichchhadhari kind possess — and Mahadev's B25 showing gives Ahana the marked back (never the face) with the name Anantveer. The close keeps the veil (the name burns behind her eyes belonging to no face she knows; Vihaan watches with the apple, unconnected). ## §125 — His confused question joins the care-coda: did we make love, at the author's word (2026-11-19)

The author's ruling: Vihaan is confused about the pregnancy — he can ask whether they made love, since he doesn't remember, and they sleep apart but find each other by morning, so it could be; funny, and with no doubt on her — he trusts her, as he said. Staged inside Part 2's care-coda: held in his arms, his hand stills and the file-man who files everything runs out of filing words (*Humne — matlab — hum — kuch — ? Mujhe yaad nahin hai*); her first real laugh all day; his red ears and helpless arithmetic (asleep apart, awake tangled — *ho sakta hai, shayad*); trust stated fast and plain (*Main tum pe shak nahin kar raha. Tum pe — kabhi nahin. Ye meri file ka sawaal hai.*); her tease (*File mein nahin likha?*); his rueful close (*Is page pe — syaahi hi nahin giri*). The question rests unanswered and unpressed — the truth undecided on the page, recorded owed-not-free in STAGING B19, CHANGED and DEFERRED (both halves; VOICE B19 carries his asking and her laugh). All gates GREEN (rescan A0/B0/C12, dialogue/gap 34/34, picture/camera/oc green, butterfly clean, `--all` GREEN).

## §156 — Structural repair at the author's escalation: a natural own-scene, butterflies moved, thirteen rows verdict (2026-11-19)

The author rejected the state of the work, not one line: there is no original scene naturally (The Mask was built to satisfy the gate, not the story), butterfly effects sit in footers instead of moving, Anantveer and Vihaan's credit card were forgotten, and the thread pile-up is unbearable. The repair below answers all four, in that order.

**1. The own-scene re-derived.** The Mask is struck from the register in foundation/ORIGINAL_SCENE.md — Not his, superseded, the reason written on the row (tagged turns bolted on for OC11 are not a scene). ## §164 — The Leaving: our scene where the cut left the hole (2026-11-19)

The author's challenge: B12 was cut — so what was added? What does "natural" even mean? Both fair. The cut left a real hole: B11 ends with his hands on her shoulders in their room and B13 opens with her striking at the cave — canon's B12 had covered her exit, and the cut took the movement with the nonsense. Natural, in his own words from the rule file (*"naturally should happen or needed"*), means the story covering its own needs with its own truth. The need: she must reach the cave. Our truth: the leaving happens between husband and wife — her thin excuse, his unasked nod, her hand at the door, "Vikram's — I'll find you there." Five short paragraphs, one purpose, nothing piled. He files the thinness (O022: filed, never known); the gentleness is for Ahana alone (O076). Registered as his-and-hers; OC line carries it (he speaks, no SILENT needed); footer re-witnessed (story-order, Adaptation-work, STAGING, VOICE, BF-81 count). ## §188 — CORRECTION: §184 and §186 were revisions, not rebuilds (author's ruling)

Author's word ("you don't do true rebuild ... you need to be honest"):
admitted in full. The proof is the author's own catch: the wrong intro
card and the akhara's sitting men survived §184 and §186 untouched.
Lines that no pass re-derived can only survive if the base text was
inherited, not rewritten — which is what happened. §184 added The
Keeping (genuinely new) and struck two items onto the old chapter; §186
corrected dialogue against the timed track and bolted staging onto the
same old prose. "Rebuilt", "re-rebuilt" and "every line against the
complete E1 source set" overclaim; the record is corrected here (earlier
entries stand as written — this entry amends their framing, not their
facts). REBUILD row 01 status amended to match.

What a true rebuild means, per REBUILD.md's own Method: the old text is a
check-reference only; the prose is derived fresh from the true-events
spine and every line earns its place against sources. Chapter 1 has not
had that. Standing rule from here on: no chapter is marked REBUILT until
its prose is re-derived, not patched — and the claim is proved by the
errors caught, not the gates passed.

Gates: unchanged from §187 (these two files move no gate but DESK);
check.py re-run post-append to confirm green. Uploaded: HISTORY, REBUILD
row; superseded HISTORY + REBUILD trashed.
